Registry versions come only from exact SemVer keys with compatible metadata; Git, local, and other sources remain `Resolution::Other`. Peer ranges and optional-peer names come from the metadata, while installed production and optional references come from the snapshot, with optional precedence. `resolve` follows an importer's exact reference, including an npm alias; `resolve_from` follows the installed reference of that snapshot. Peer contexts are never stripped for lookup. Their suffix is stripped only for joining metadata. Ordered indexes and binary search keep lookup and validation near-linear in input size.

Local `link:` references, including the valid self-reference `link:`, stay available through `pnpm_reference` and return no locked package/version. A selected workspace member resolves through the root lockfile's importer with `load_workspace` (section 30). Paths, linked contents, and package scripts are never executed or opened by this model.

**Schema and errors.** Mapping keys must resolve to scalar strings; complex keys, duplicates, incompatible tags or scalar types, malformed modeled fields, and missing referenced snapshots reject the entire model (`FS1026`). The YAML syntax layer itself still preserves complex and duplicate keys and every document. Anchors and aliases may share schema-compatible data without expansion. Duplicate scalar keys also produce the established review finding `FS1021`, with an explanation that the YAML model is rejected, rather than JSON's last-wins explanation. Unknown format versions produce `FS1010`; YAML syntax and resource-limit failures produce `FS2001`. Unknown metadata fields do not establish package facts. A stream containing several documents is rejected as a structure mismatch, even if each separately declares 9.0; the newer multi-document environment-lockfile variant has not been adopted.

**Tests and fixtures.** Facade tests in `crates/frameshift-engine/tests/pnpm.rs` cover aliases, importer-specific versions, distinct peer contexts with different transitive versions, optional dependencies and peers, local/self links, compatible YAML properties, unknown versions, stale references, invalid scalar types, duplicate keys, and multi-document rejection. A loader test proves selected-files-only snapshot reads, that peer-only manifest declarations do not falsely mark pnpm stale, and node/byte bounds. Three additional fictional product cases (`package-pnpm`, `package-pnpm-errors`, `package-pnpm-limits`) extend the package fixture set to nine. Their golden updater is unchanged. `package-manager-conflicts` now reports the existing incomplete pnpm lockfile as `FS1026`, rather than unimplemented-format `FS1010`; its source is unchanged. Fixture metadata now permits `yaml.max-depth` and `yaml.max-nodes`.

**External comparison (2026-10-19).** The official [pnpm 10.20.0 lockfile](https://github.com/pnpm/pnpm/blob/24918d2f0020c2d9e2890afeb16e38dd4983952d/pnpm-lock.yaml), kept outside the repository, parses with 197 importers and 1,800 snapshots. An external Node 26.7.0 script using pinned `yaml` 2.9.1 independently decoded it and derived every importer and snapshot lookup by exact recorded reference, with dependency-group precedence. All 6,362 expected keys, unresolved local links, and importer reference texts match. This checks syntax and reference lookup on that pinned data; no pnpm repository code was installed or executed, and no third-party fixture is committed. The opt-in test uses `FRAMESHIFT_PNPM_LOCKFILE` and optionally `FRAMESHIFT_PNPM_ORACLE` for a JSON array of `[scope, context, name, reference, expectedKeyOrNull]`, then `cargo test -p frameshift-engine --offline --locked --test pnpm external_reference_lockfile -- --ignored --nocapture`. This is a development reference check, not product execution or a general real-world evaluation.

The `yaml` fuzz target additionally interprets accepted streams as pnpm models, checking deterministic results, error spans, sorted identities, and resolution contracts. Local and native validation evidence follows below; no remote CI run is implied by local checks.

**Validation (2026-10-19).** The final Windows x86_64 debug and release workspace suites each pass 393 tests, with zero failures and five explicitly ignored golden/external runners. Workspace build, formatting, all-target Clippy with warnings denied, the dependency/boundary/version/Action-pin policy and its 13 regression cases, all 3,000 generated corpus contracts, E2 goldens, and Markdown links/style pass. The pnpm reference and all 6,362 oracle lookups pass separately. A 120-second coverage-instrumented run on the final pnpm model completed 3,849,160 executions, 10,684 features, and 2,450 saved inputs without findings. This is a smoke run, not a CPU-hour budget. Static musl builds of the YAML, pnpm, package, package-fixture, and limit tests also pass under Linux x86_64 in WSL, with byte-identical package goldens. Native six-platform CI for this development branch has not run.

**YAML boundary regressions (2026-10-19).** Independent checks with `yaml` 2.9.1 and the YAML 1.2.2 grammar additionally cover colon-prefixed plain flow keys versus empty keys, empty-key flow pairs, document-prefix BOMs with multiple block entries, future 1.x minor version directives, and paired UTF-16 escapes in quoted JSON-compatible scalars. These are preserved without changing values; internal unquoted BOMs and unpaired surrogates are rejected. The pinned 402-case suite remains green. Unicode and flow boundary seeds are retained in the in-house fuzzer.

**Long flow-line regression.** The parser caches the last scanned line boundary so repeated column queries do not rescan a long flow-line prefix for every scalar. The same regression test with 20,000 marker-like plain scalars on one 100,002-byte line took 0.73 seconds in a release-mode isolated export of `4d138da` with the new test copied in, and 0.00 seconds at the test runner's displayed precision after the cache. These are desktop test timings, not a reference-hardware performance gate. A short multiline case also checks cursor restoration after lookahead. The cache has constant size and changes no syntax result.

**Final boundary validation (2026-10-19).** After these regressions and the line cache, Windows debug and release workspace suites each pass 395 tests (zero failures, five ignored runners). The 402-case YAML suite and 6,362 pnpm reference lookups pass in release mode; relevant static musl facade and golden tests pass under WSL. All-target warnings-denied Clippy passes. A final 60-second coverage-instrumented smoke run on this code completed 1,955,495 executions, 10,294 features, and 2,201 saved inputs without findings. Earlier smoke counts above describe their respective revisions.

## 29. Yarn lockfiles (`D-072`)

`load_package` reads a selected `yarn.lock` through `Snapshot::read`, under `package.max-lockfile-bytes` and UTF-8 validation. A file whose leading `#` comment lines, after an optional byte order mark, include `# yarn lockfile v1` is classic: this is the expression `^(#.*(\r?\n))*?#\s+yarn\s+lockfile\s+v1\r?\n` that Yarn Berry's reader tests, matched case-insensitively with JavaScript's `\s` and `.`. Every other `yarn.lock` is parsed as YAML (section 27) and read as a Berry lockfile. No other file, configuration, or workspace member is opened.

**Classic syntax.** `frameshift_engine::syntax::parse_yarn_lockfile` (`crates/frameshift-syntax/src/yarn.rs`) reproduces the tokenizer and parser of Yarn 1.22.22, `src/lockfile/parse.js` in its release bundle. Tokens are newlines (CR, LF, or CRLF, where Yarn consumes a following LF with any newline, so a blank line of two LFs is one token), `#` comments to the next LF, indentation in steps of two spaces after an LF or CRLF only, quoted strings, digit runs, the prefixes `true` and `false`, `:`, `,`, and unquoted words that start with an ASCII letter, `/`, `.`, or `-` and run to a colon, space, CR, LF, or comma. A quoted string ends at the first quote that does not follow exactly one backslash, and its text must then be one JSON string, as `JSON.parse` reads it. Objects nest by indentation; a member has one or more comma-separated keys that share one value, an optional colon, and a string, digit run, boolean, or nested object. The parser is iterative and follows the control flow of Yarn's recursive `parse`, including how nested objects end. It rejects, as Yarn does, odd indentation, tokens the grammar does not allow, empty keys, and a comment `yarn lockfile v<n>` with `n` above 1 (trimmed with JavaScript's `trim`). It also rejects what Yarn would read differently: a file that contains the three Git merge conflict markers, which Yarn merges, top-level indentation, after which Yarn returns early and ignores the rest of the file, and escapes of unpaired surrogates, which `JSON.parse` accepts. A leading byte order mark is skipped.

`YarnDocument` borrows the `SourceText` and holds flat value, member, and key arenas with exact spans; decoded strings are stored only when they contain escapes. `YarnObject::members` lists members in source order, `get` returns the value of a key's last occurrence, as Yarn does, and `duplicate_keys` lists every shadowed key, including a key that one member repeats. Digit runs keep their exact text. `YarnLimits` supplies `yarn.max-depth` (default 64 nested objects, counting the top-level object) and `yarn.max-nodes` (default 4,194,304 values and keys); exceeding either is an error. A `YarnError` names a kind and a span, never source content.

**Classic model.** `Lockfile::from_yarn_classic` (`crates/frameshift-package/src/lockfile/yarn_classic.rs`) reads every top-level member as an entry keyed by its patterns joined with `, `. A pattern splits as Yarn's `normalizePattern` does: the name ends at the first `@` after an optional leading `@`, and a pattern without a range requests `latest`. All patterns of an entry must name the same package and agree on an `npm:` alias, whose target is the installed name unless the entry records `name`. `version` is required; `resolved`, `integrity`, `uid`, `registry`, and `name` must be strings and `permissions` and `prebuiltVariants` objects when present; other fields are ignored. An entry resolves to `Resolution::Registry` when its version is exact SemVer, every pattern requests a range or tag (also behind an alias), and `resolved` is absent or an HTTP URL; otherwise `Resolution::Other` keeps the resolved URL, or the first range. `dependencies` and `optionalDependencies` merge, optional last. Duplicate keys, unexpected types, and a dependency `<name>@<range>` without its own entry reject the whole lockfile. The model records no workspaces, because Yarn 1 does not.

**Berry model.** `Lockfile::from_yarn_berry` (`crates/frameshift-package/src/lockfile/yarn_berry.rs`) reads exactly one YAML document with `__metadata`; `version` must be the canonical decimal 4, 5, 6, 8, 9, or 10, the versions that stable Yarn releases write: 4 (2.x and 3.0), 5 (3.1), 6 (3.2 to 3.8), 8 (4.0 to 4.13), 9 (4.14), and 10 (4.15 and later), as each release's `LOCKFILE_VERSION` shows. Values follow Yarn's js-yaml failsafe schema: every scalar is a string, an empty plain scalar is null, and only the `!`, `!!str`, and `!!map` tags are accepted; aliases are read as references. Mapping keys must be distinct strings. Each entry needs `resolution`, a strict locator `<ident>@<reference>` whose reference is not `unknown`, and `linkType` `hard` or `soft`; `version`, `languageName`, `checksum`, and `conditions` must be strings, `bin` a string or a map of strings, `dependencies` and `peerDependencies` maps of package names to strings, and `dependenciesMeta` and `peerDependenciesMeta` maps whose `built`, `optional`, and `unplugged` flags are `true`, `1`, `false`, or `0`, as Yarn's `parseBoolean` reads them. Unknown fields are ignored. An entry is keyed by its resolution locator, which must be unique. Its key splits at commas and the spaces around them into descriptors, which must have a range and may be claimed by one entry only; formats up to 6 normalize them (below). `npm:<exact version>` resolves to `Resolution::Registry`, whose recorded `version` must agree, `workspace:<path>` to `Resolution::Workspace` with `.` as the empty root path, and everything else to `Resolution::Other` with the locator. A workspace entry also becomes a `LockedWorkspace` with its path and name, without the `0.0.0-use.local` version marker that Yarn records for every workspace; its production declarations hold the dependencies that Yarn merges from the workspace's production, optional, and development declarations, and its peer declarations stay separate. Dependencies are not required to have entries (below).

**Lookups.** `Lockfile::resolve_declared(workspace, name, spec)` resolves a manifest declaration; for npm, Bun, and pnpm it equals `resolve`. Yarn classic looks for the bare-name pattern, which `yarn add` once wrote, and then for `<name>@<spec>`, as Yarn 1's install does; `resolve` returns `None` for classic lockfiles, and `resolve_from` looks up `<name>@<range>` of the package's dependency. Yarn Berry builds the descriptor that Yarn looks up: `normalizeDependency` turns a range without a colon that node-semver's default mode accepts (section 26), or a tag of ASCII letters, digits, `.`, `_`, and `-` not starting with `v`, into `npm:<range>`; a path that starts with a drive letter and separator, `/`, `./`, or `../` becomes `file:<path>`; and a `file:`, `link:`, or `portal:` range without `::` is bound to the declaring package with `::locator=` and its locator, percent-encoded as Node's `querystring.escape` does. `resolve` uses the range that the workspace's entry records, `resolve_declared` the manifest's range, and `resolve_from` the range of the package's dependency, each bound to the workspace's or package's locator. Other protocols, such as `patch:`, `exec:`, `catalog:`, Git, and URLs, are looked up as written. Yarn replaces descriptors that the root's `resolutions` or a catalog select before it writes the lockfile, so their original descriptors have no entry and stay unresolved; the whole lockfile is not rejected for that. `.yarnrc.yml` is never read, so the default protocol is taken to be `npm:`.

**Staleness and generation.** `out_of_sync` lists, for Yarn classic, the production, development, and optional kinds with a declaration that has no pattern; peers are not installed by Yarn 1 and are excluded. Yarn 1 locks no workspace package, so a declaration that a workspace satisfies counts as missing, which the `FS1027` explanation states. For Yarn Berry, the effective declarations (production, then optional, then development, each overriding the previous one) are compared with the root workspace's recorded dependencies after normalization, and the peer declarations with the recorded peers after Yarn's replacement of a range that is neither `workspace:` nor valid with `*`. After a lockfile is read, `FS1029` (review) reports a Yarn lockfile when `packageManager` names Yarn 1 and the lockfile is Berry, which Yarn 1 ignores, or Yarn 2 or later and the lockfile is classic, which the next install migrates.

**Findings.** A classic syntax error, merge conflict markers, and a classic limit produce `FS2001`; a newer classic version, a Berry version outside the list, and a YAML `yarn.lock` without `__metadata` produce `FS1010`; duplicate keys produce `FS1021` with `FS1026`; structural deviations produce `FS1026`. The explanations name kinds, limits, and byte ranges only.

**Tests and fixtures.** Nine unit tests in `crates/frameshift-syntax/src/yarn/tests.rs` cover the written format, CRLF, byte order marks, empty input, every tokenizer quirk, JSON string decoding, rejections, merge conflicts and version comments, duplicate keys, limits, and errors without source text. `crates/frameshift-engine/tests/yarn.rs` parses through the facade, and `tests/yarn_lockfile.rs` has seven facade tests with hand-written fictional lockfiles in the structure that Yarn 1.22.22, 2.4.3, 3.8.7, and 4.18.1 write: classic pattern lookups, aliases, local sources, bare-name patterns, staleness, and rejections; Berry workspaces, aliases, bound paths, normalized declarations, formats up to 6, effective-declaration staleness, versions, failsafe scalars, and rejections. Three product fixtures extend the package cases to twelve: `package-yarn-classic`, `package-yarn-berry` (format 10 with a workspace member and format 6), and `package-yarn-errors` (ten directories with every rejection, `FS1029`, and classic staleness, under a lowered `yarn.max-nodes`). The fixture schema and harness accept `yarn.max-depth` and `yarn.max-nodes`, and the harness renders declarations through `resolve_declared`.

**External comparison (2026-10-20).** Three opt-in runners in the engine tests compare the implementation with Yarn itself; their inputs stay outside the repository, and no Yarn code or third-party lockfile is committed.

- *Classic syntax.* The tokenizer and parser module of Yarn 1.22.22 (BSD-2-Clause), copied unchanged from the release bundle that Corepack downloaded into a script outside the repository, parsed 500,000 generated lockfile-like texts and mutations (ten seeds). All 329,186 texts that Yarn reads as written produce the same values; the 161,935 texts that Yarn rejects, the 5,548 that Yarn truncates at top-level indentation, the 3,327 with unpaired surrogate escapes, and the 4 with merge conflict markers are rejected. Run with `FRAMESHIFT_YARN_ORACLE` naming the generated JSON lines files: `cargo test -p frameshift-engine --offline --locked --release --test yarn external_yarn_classic_oracle -- --ignored --nocapture`.
- *Installs.* A fictional workspace (a root and two members; registry ranges, a tag, an npm alias, optional, development, and peer declarations, `file:`, `link:`, and `portal:` sources, and workspace siblings) was installed with Yarn 1.22.22, 2.4.3, 3.0.2, 3.1.1, 3.8.7, 4.13.0, 4.14.1, and 4.18.1, which wrote formats 1, 4, 4, 5, 6, 8, 9, and 10, using small public npm packages and with scripts disabled. For each of the 15 or 16 declarations per project, `resolve_declared` returns the package that the install placed where Node.js finds it, or a local source for a local declaration; Yarn 1 records no entry only for workspace siblings. Every dependency of every entry resolves, and only the classic project reports `FS1027`, for its workspace sibling. Run with `FRAMESHIFT_YARN_PROJECTS` naming the installed project directories, each with an `oracle.json` from the recording script: `cargo test -p frameshift-engine --offline --locked --test yarn_lockfile external_reference_projects -- --ignored --nocapture`.
- *Large lockfiles.* Yarn's own repository lockfiles, [yarnpkg/berry at `@yarnpkg/cli/4.18.1`](https://github.com/yarnpkg/berry/blob/75ebe063092f86e47e4afeaf1ae4bdf24402874b/yarn.lock) (format 10, 810,106 bytes, 49 workspaces, 2,108 packages) and [yarnpkg/yarn at `v1.22.22`](https://github.com/yarnpkg/yarn/blob/6b251d2374c4e42b41ce5782d181bb53a3bd3a9a/yarn.lock) (classic, 313,430 bytes, 1,062 entries), and the format 6 lockfile of the approved real-world sample (`D-043`, 1,178 packages), parse. For each of their 4,958, 1,981, and 2,598 entry dependencies, `resolve_from` returns the entry that a script derives with Yarn's own readers: `parseSyml` of `@yarnpkg/parsers` 3.1.0 with node-semver 7.8.5 for Berry and the extracted 1.22.22 parser for classic. The 11 unresolved dependencies in Berry's lockfile come from `resolutions` and a catalog and are unresolved in both. The script restates the normalization and binding rules in JavaScript, so this comparison checks parsing and indexing on real data, while the installs above check the rules. Run with `FRAMESHIFT_YARN_LOCKFILES` naming lockfiles that have `<lockfile>.oracle` files beside them: `cargo test -p frameshift-engine --offline --locked --release --test yarn_lockfile external_reference_lockfiles -- --ignored --nocapture`.

**Fuzzing.** The seventeenth target, `yarn`, checks classic lockfiles for deterministic results, valid value, member, and key spans, an effective node bound, key lookups, the classic model's determinism and resolution contracts, and that writing the values in Yarn's layout and parsing them again gives the same values. The `yaml` target now also runs the Berry model on every accepted stream, and the shared lockfile contract checks that declared and transitive resolutions are locked packages. Coverage-instrumented Windows smoke runs on the final code completed 4,679,157 `yarn` executions (8,988 features, 2,012 saved inputs) in 120 seconds, 2,021,030 `yaml` executions (10,637 features, 2,262 inputs) in 60 seconds, and 2,413,958 `package` executions (9,374 features, 1,938 inputs) in 60 seconds, all without findings; every saved input passes replay in an ordinary release build. These are smoke runs, not CPU-hour budgets.

**Limitations.** `patch:`, `exec:`, and catalog descriptors and descriptors that `resolutions` replaced stay unresolved; Yarn 4's migration rewrite of patch descriptors in formats up to 6 is not reproduced, so lookups follow the writing release instead. `load_package` still reports classic declarations that a workspace satisfies as stale; `load_workspace` links them to the satisfying sibling instead (section 30). Berry releases before 4.18 bundle earlier node-semver 7 versions than the 7.8.5 semantics of section 26. YAML 1.2 and js-yaml differ in corner cases; accepted lockfiles pass the structural checks, and other differences are rejected rather than guessed.

**Validation (2026-10-20).** On Windows x86_64 with Rust 1.98.0, the debug and release workspace suites each pass 413 tests with zero failures; the eight ignored tests are the three golden updaters and the five external runners. The offline locked build, formatting, all-target Clippy with warnings denied, the crate-boundary, dependency, version, and Action-pin checker with its 13 regression cases, the E2 goldens, and all 3,000 generated corpus contracts pass, and the existing discovery and package goldens are unchanged. Linux test runs in WSL were not repeated for this increment, and native six-platform CI for this development branch has not run.

## 30. Workspaces (`D-073`)

Implemented under the owner-confirmed `D-073` in `crates/frameshift-package/src/workspace.rs` and its submodules, exposed through `frameshift_engine::package`. No dependency is added, and every file is read through `Snapshot::read`.

**Loading.** `load_workspace(snapshot, limits, findings)` loads the root package directory (section 26) without its staleness check, reads `pnpm-workspace.yaml` when it is admitted, takes the `workspaces` patterns of the root `package.json` with their exact text locations, selects the releases to evaluate (below), evaluates their declarations over discovery's inventory, reads the `package.json` of each candidate directory at most once, and checks the root lockfile for staleness of the root and of every member. The result `Workspace` holds the root `PackageDirectory`, the declaration files read, the evaluated `WorkspaceSemantics`, the members with their manifests sorted by path, the undetermined directories, and `complete`. A root without a declaration that its package manager reads is a single package with no members. `Workspace::select(None | Some(directory))` returns a `SelectedPackage` for the root or a member, or `SelectionError::NotAMember` or `Undetermined`; `SelectedPackage::resolve_declared` resolves through the root lockfile at the member's workspace path, `lockfile_workspace` names that path, and `sibling` finds the root or another member with a given `name` (`D-022`). `release_members` evaluates one release at the root, and `select_members` evaluates one release over a `ListedDirectory` listing without a snapshot.

**Releases.** `WorkspaceSemantics` names eight reference releases: `Npm` (npm 11.11.0 with `@npmcli/map-workspaces` 5.0.3, glob 13.0.6, and minimatch 10.2.5), `YarnClassic` (Yarn 1.22.22, glob 7), `YarnBerryV2` (Yarn 2.4.3 and 3.8.7, globby over fast-glob 3.2), `YarnBerryV4` (Yarn 4.18.1, fast-glob 3.3), `PnpmV10` (pnpm 10.34.7, tinyglobby), `PnpmV11` (pnpm 11.28.6, tinyglobby), `PnpmV12` (pnpm 12.14.0, wax), and `Bun` (Bun 1.4.0 and 1.4.3). A `packageManager` major selects one of them; another major adds `FS1037` and evaluates every modeled release of its manager. Without the field, the detected lockfile's manager decides; a Yarn Berry lockfile of format 4 to 6 means Yarn 2 and 3, format 8 to 10 Yarn 4, and pnpm evaluates its three releases. Without a detected manager, every release whose declaration exists is evaluated. pnpm reads only `pnpm-workspace.yaml`; npm, Yarn, and Bun read only `workspaces`; a declaration that the detected manager does not read is reported (`FS1034`).

**Pattern grammar.** `src/workspace/pattern.rs` parses one pattern into segments of tokens (literal characters, `*`, `?`, ASCII classes with ranges and `!`) and brace alternatives, or rejects it with a `PatternRejection` (`FS1030`); the grammar and the rejected forms are listed in `D-073`. Braces expand within a segment, so expansion never multiplies across segments, bounded by `workspace.max-alternatives`. Yarn 1 reads a leading `!` as a literal character. `src/workspace/matcher.rs` matches segments with `*` backtracking and paths with a dynamic program over segment positions, charging `workspace.max-match-work`; a wildcard, a class, or `**` never matches a name that starts with `.`, except picomatch's classes at a segment start (Yarn 2 and later, pnpm 10 and 11). JavaScript matchers compare UTF-16 code units, so `?` does not match an astral character there; wax and Bun compare scalar values.

**Semantics.** `src/workspace/semantics.rs` reproduces each release on the inventory:

- **npm 11:** a negation is dropped when a later positive pattern matches it as text, and a positive pattern is dropped when a remaining negation matches it as text (minimatch text matching: a trailing `**` needs a segment, a trailing `/` of the text is tolerated, and a trailing `/` of the negation is required). A directory matches a positive pattern with a trailing `/`, no segment is `node_modules`, and no negation ignores it with glob's ignore semantics (`dot: true`, and a negation ending in `/**` also ignores the directory and its children). A member's name falls back to its directory name; duplicate names and invalid manifests make npm fail.
- **Yarn 1:** patterns become `<pattern>/+(package.json|yarn.json)` with the ignore `<pattern>/node_modules/**/...`; a member needs a `name` and a `version`, a non-private root with patterns fails, duplicate names fail, and a directory that a `./` pattern and a plain pattern both select fails as a duplicate.
- **Yarn 2 and 3:** globby removes repeated patterns, pairs each positive pattern with the negations after it, and reads it with fast-glob 3.2: a static pattern is looked up, a dynamic one is walked below its glob-parent base directory, entries match without a trailing-slash retry, and a trailing `**` matches no segment only when no `*` precedes it.
- **Yarn 4:** fast-glob 3.3 expands braces, separates static from dynamic patterns, and walks dynamic ones below their bases; when one dynamic pattern has no base, all dynamic patterns are read from the root. Entries match with a trailing-slash retry, negations exclude entries with `dot: true`, and negations whose basename is static or that end in `/**` stop the walk at directories they match. `**/node_modules`, `**/.git`, and `**/.yarn` are such negations; their `**` does not cross dot directories. A static `a/` keeps its `/`, which only a negation ending in exactly `*` or matching the slash excludes.
- **Yarn 2 and later:** each member's own `workspaces` is evaluated below it, recursively.
- **pnpm 10, 11, and 12:** a pattern selects the directories whose `package.json`, `package.yaml`, or `package.json5` it matches; negations exclude that manifest only, not the subtree; `node_modules` and `bower_components` segments exclude a directory, in pnpm 10 and 11 only when no dot directory precedes them. Negations match dot names in pnpm 11 and 12. pnpm 12 also hands negations ending in `**` to the wax walk of each pattern that is neither literal nor `parent/*`, which matches them against directories and the manifest file both relative to the pattern's literal prefix and relative to the root. The root is always a project and not a member; duplicate names are allowed.
- **Bun 1.4:** a pattern without glob syntax lists a directory directly, bypassing ignores and negations, and fails when the directory or its `package.json` is missing. A glob selects `package.json` files below the root, skipping `node_modules`, `.git`, and `CMakeFiles`, and only later negations exclude a match (a trailing `**` needs a segment, and negations with a trailing `/` or a leading `./` exclude nothing). A negated pattern is also walked as a pattern of its own with its first segment inverted: `!a` adds every other top-level package, and `!./a` walks one level deeper. A member needs a non-empty `name`.

A pattern that could reach an entry that discovery did not traverse (an ignored directory, a link, a reparse point, an unreadable directory) is reported at that entry (`FS1031`), unless the release's own ignores exclude it; an incomplete inventory is reported once at project scope. Release-specific rejections (`PatternRejection::Quirk`) cover the forms listed in `D-073`.

**Aggregation and findings.** A directory is a member only when every evaluated release selects it; directories on which they differ are undetermined (`FS1032`), as are directories whose manifest is hidden or only `yarn.json`, `package.yaml`, or `package.json5` (`FS1036`). A rejected pattern (`FS1030`), an incomplete declaration, or a limit (`FS1038`) leaves no member determined. Conditions under which a release fails are reported per release (`FS1035`). The case sensitivity check evaluates each exact release again with Unicode lowercase folding, from manifest presence only, and reports a different candidate set (`FS1033`). pnpm's `pnpm-workspace.yaml` must be one mapping document whose `packages` is null, missing, or a sequence of non-empty strings; a plain scalar that YAML could read as another type counts as no string (`FS1035`), and duplicate keys are reported (`FS1021`). Findings name paths, fields, and releases, never package names or patterns.

**Staleness.** `Lockfile::out_of_sync_in(workspace, manifest, linked)` compares any workspace's declarations; `load_workspace` checks the root and each member, reporting `FS1027` at the lockfile for the root and at the member's `package.json` for a member. A Yarn 1 declaration that the root or a member satisfies by name and version is linked, not stale.

**Limits.** `WorkspaceLimits` holds `workspace.max-patterns` (1,024 patterns per declaration), `workspace.max-pattern-bytes` (4,096), `workspace.max-alternatives` (256 per segment and, for Yarn 4, per pattern), and `workspace.max-match-work` (100,000,000 steps per release); `LoadLimits` carries them, and the limit set has 39 entries (section 21).

**Differential validation (2026-10-20).** Outside the repository, `run.mjs` materialized fictional workspaces once per tool and recorded what npm 11.11.0 (`map-workspaces` directly), Yarn 1.22.22 (`workspaces info`), Yarn 2.4.3, 3.8.7, and 4.18.1 (`workspaces list`), pnpm 10.34.7, 11.28.6, and 12.14.0 (`ls -r`), and Bun 1.4.0 and 1.4.3 (`install --lockfile-only`) select, on Windows x86_64 with Node.js 26.7.0; scripts were never run and no registry package was installed. 187 hand-written probes and 7,900 generated cases (seeds 1 to 4, with patterns built from the core grammar and rejected forms, trees with dot, ignored, and non-ASCII names, missing names and versions, invalid and alternative manifests, nested declarations, and non-private roots) gave 62,796 comparisons of an exact release result with a tool's listing, all equal; 17,431 further comparisons were skipped because the model reported its result as not exact. Each model correction was rechecked on every earlier set, and the last seed was generated after the last correction but one. The opt-in test reads the recorded cases and listings:

```text
FRAMESHIFT_WORKSPACE_CASES=<cases.json> FRAMESHIFT_WORKSPACE_RESULTS=<results.json> cargo test -p frameshift-engine --offline --locked --test workspace external_workspace_oracle -- --ignored --nocapture
```

On the twelve projects of the approved real-world sample (`D-043`), the five workspaces resolve completely: 71 members with pnpm 12, 12 members agreed on by pnpm 10, 11, and 12 (with `FS1037` for an older pnpm major), and three workspaces of 6, 6, and 0 members; a pnpm root that also declares `workspaces` reports `FS1034`.

**Tests and fixtures.** Eight unit tests in `src/workspace/tests.rs` cover the accepted grammar, every rejection, Yarn 1's literal `!`, the dot rule and character units, globstars, Bun's inverted walk, brace expansion and glob-parent, and the work bound. Eight facade tests in `crates/frameshift-engine/tests/workspace.rs` cover npm members with root lockfile resolution and siblings, disputed members without a package manager, rejected patterns and ignored directories, Yarn 1 sibling linking and member staleness, nested Yarn 4 workspaces, the `FS1034`, `FS1035`, and `FS1037` findings, case dependence, and single packages. Eight product fixtures of kind `workspace` below `fixtures/discovery/` (npm, pnpm, Yarn 1, Yarn 4, Bun, and adversarial cases for disagreeing releases, rejected patterns, and `workspace.max-patterns`) render into `expected/workspace.txt`, updated only with `cargo test -p frameshift-engine --offline --locked --test workspace_fixtures -- --ignored update_workspace_goldens`; their fictional trees produce the same members with the corresponding reference releases.

**Fuzzing.** The eighteenth target, `workspace`, evaluates every release over listings decoded from the input and checks repeatable results, independence of the listing order, members only among listed directories with a manifest, the name and version that Yarn 1 and Bun require, no members after a rejected pattern, and equal members under a smaller work bound unless that bound is reported. A coverage-instrumented Windows smoke run on the final code completed 353,699 executions (10,378 features, 2,297 saved inputs) in 180 seconds without findings; this is a smoke run, not a CPU-hour budget.

**Limitations.** Other releases of a modeled generation are assumed to read patterns like its reference release. pnpm-managed store and state directories inside the workspace are not modeled. `yarn.json`, `package.yaml`, and `package.json5` are not read. Links are never followed, and case-insensitive file systems are reported rather than modeled. The generated cases avoid case variants, because the tools ran on a case-insensitive file system. pnpm 12's two-document lockfile, which section 28 rejects, leaves member versions unresolved.

## 31. Next.js detection (`D-074`, `D-076`)

Implemented under the owner-confirmed `D-074`, amended by `D-076`, in `crates/frameshift-nextjs` (adapter ID `nextjs`), exposed through `frameshift_engine::nextjs`. `detect(inventory, selected, findings)` returns a `Detection` for a `SelectedPackage` (section 30) and reads no file content.

**Evidence.** The `next` declaration is taken from production, development, optional, or peer dependencies, in this order; an `npm:` alias of another package is not `next`. The verdict is `NextJs` for a non-peer declaration with a configuration file or at least one route file, `Inconclusive` (`FS1040`) for a declaration without either or a configuration file in the package directory without a declaration, and `Absent` otherwise, without findings. A configuration file above the package directory serves the packages below it and is no evidence on its own (`D-076`).

**Version.** `VersionEvidence::Locked` holds the version that the root lockfile resolves for the package at its workspace path, when the resolved package is `next` from the registry; a non-registry resolution gives `Unknown`. Without a resolution, the declared range's lowest version is a `LowerBound`, or `Unknown` for `0.0.0`, tags, and other sources; both report `FS1042`, which suggests running on the workspace root when no lockfile was read. A major outside 14 to 16 reports `FS1041` (`D-023`).

**Configuration.** From the package directory up to the root, the first directory with a name that the version loads wins, in the order `next.config.js`, `next.config.mjs`, `next.config.ts` (Next.js 15 and later), and `next.config.mts` (Next.js 16, `ConfigLoad::DependsOnNode`); otherwise the nearest rejected name (`next.config.ts` before 15, `.cjs`, `.cts`, `.json`, `.jsx`, `.tsx`) is `Unsupported`. An unknown version is read as Next.js 16. A name counts when Node.js would find a regular file there, as `findUp` does with its default `type: "file"` and `stat`, which follows links (`D-076`): an admitted or oversized file, or a link whose known target inside the root is one; a directory, an untraversed or ignored directory, a special file, a dangling link, and a link to the root do not count; a link that leaves the root or is unresolved, an ignored entry that is not a directory, and an uninspectable entry may be a file and count. `FS1043` reports an unsupported name or `.mts` (review) and the absence of a configuration inside the root (info). Next.js 14.2.35 fails on `next.config.ts`, `.jsx`, `.tsx`, and `.json` and ignores `.cjs` and `.cts`; Next.js 15 and 16 fail on `.cjs`, `.cts`, `.json`, `.jsx`, and `.tsx`, and 15 also on `.mts`.

**Routers.** `app` and `pages` are the first existing entry of `<package>/<name>` and `<package>/src/<name>`, as `findPagesDir` checks with `fs.existsSync`, under which a dangling link is missing. A traversed directory counts its route files with the default page extensions `tsx`, `ts`, `jsx`, and `js`: `page` and `route` files outside folders that start with `_` below `app`, and page files except `_app`, `_document`, `_error`, and `.d.ts` files below `pages`. `RouterState::Other` marks an entry that is not a traversed directory; `complete: false` marks a directory with untraversed entries below it; both report `FS1044`, as do `app` and `pages` in different directories from Next.js 15 or with an unknown version, and a `src` entry that is not a traversed directory, such as a link, when a router directory is missing in the package directory, also for an inconclusive verdict (`D-076`). `Detection::router` returns `App`, `Pages`, `Hybrid`, or `None`.

**Ignored entries (`D-076`).** For a Next.js verdict, `Detection::ignored` lists, in path order, the entries of the package directory that Next.js ignores: every entry with one of the nine configuration file names other than the selected file, unless the selected name is rejected, and `src/app` or `src/pages` beside the directory of the same name in the package directory. Each is reported with `FS1045` (info), whose explanation distinguishes a shadowed file, an entry that is not a regular file, and a router directory below `src`. Entries above the package directory are not listed, so a configuration that several members share is reported once, for its own package.

**Tests.** A unit test checks the router kinds. Seven facade tests in `crates/frameshift-engine/tests/nextjs.rs` build projects at test time, so no committed manifest names the real `next` package (`Q-014`): an App Router project with a pnpm lockfile, private folders, and a rejected `src/pages` beside `app`; a workspace member resolved through the root npm lockfile with its configuration in the workspace root; a Pages Router project without a lockfile; versions and configuration names outside what Next.js supports; and incomplete, absent, and partly ignored evidence. Under `D-076`, the sixth covers ignored configuration names and router directories, directories named like configuration files, and a member below the root's configuration without a declaration, and the seventh, on Unix only, covers a dangling `app` link and a `src` link. The opt-in `external_projects` test prints the workspace and Next.js detection of every package of the directories in `FRAMESHIFT_NEXTJS_PROJECTS`:

```text
FRAMESHIFT_NEXTJS_PROJECTS=<dir>[;<dir>...] cargo test -p frameshift-engine --offline --release --test nextjs external_projects -- --ignored --nocapture
```

On the approved real-world sample (`D-043`), 28 packages are detected as Next.js: 26 with App Router route files, the Next.js 13 project with both routers (`FS1041`), and one example with only a configuration file. Locked versions range from 14.3.0-canary.57 to 16.4.0; the 16 applications of the pnpm 12 workspace keep the lower bound of their ranges (`FS1042`), because its two-document lockfile is not read. Library members that declare `next` and a sparse checkout without route files are inconclusive (`FS1040`).

**Validation (2026-10-20).** On Windows x86_64 with Rust 1.98.0, the debug and release workspace suites each pass 436 tests with zero failures; the eleven ignored tests are the four golden updaters and seven external runners. The offline locked build, formatting, all-target Clippy with warnings denied, the crate-boundary, dependency, version, and Action-pin checker with its 13 regression cases, the E2 goldens, and all 3,000 generated corpus contracts pass, and the existing discovery and package goldens are unchanged. The refactoring commit of the package loader builds and passes the package and engine tests on its own. Linux test runs in WSL were not repeated for this increment; native six-platform CI covers them.

**Limitations.** `pageExtensions` and other configuration values are not evaluated; route files with other extensions are not counted. A configuration above the analyzed root is not visible. The rules follow the Next.js 14.2.35, 15.5.28, and 16.4.2 sources; the `D-076` lookup rules were checked against the compiled `find-up`, `loadConfig`, and `findPagesDir` of the Next.js 14.2.35, 15.5.18, and 16.2.9 distributions. Next.js itself was not run. An entry of unknown type, such as a link that leaves the root, counts as a configuration file. A rejected name in the package directory is preferred over a rejected name above it, although Next.js 14 fails on the nearest name of its own rejected list.

## 32. Inspection (`D-075`, `D-076`)

Implemented under the owner-confirmed `D-075`, with the adversarial fixtures and escaping of `D-076`, in `crates/frameshift-engine/src/inspect.rs` and `src/document.rs`, with the command in `crates/frameshift-cli`. No dependency is added.

**Pipeline.** `inspect(root, &InspectOptions { limits, packages })` validates the limits, takes a snapshot (section 24), loads the workspace (section 30), and runs Next.js detection (section 31) for each selected package: with `PackageScope::All` the root and then every determined member in path order, with `Root` the root, with `Member(path)` one member. A path that is not a determined member fails with `InspectError::Selection`. After detection, unreadable files are reported (`FS1019`), and the findings are finalized. The result `Inspection` holds a `FileSummary` (completeness, the stopping limit, admitted files and bytes, traversed directories, ignored entries per reason, oversized files, links, reparse points, special files, untraversed directories, uninspectable and unrepresentable entries), the `Workspace` with the root's package manager and lockfile, one `PackageInspection` per package (directory, `name`, manifest status, `Detection`), and the `FindingList`. Inspection reads directory listings, `.gitignore` files, `package.json` files, the selected lockfile, and `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, each at most once, and no other file content; it computes no snapshot identity. `InspectError::is_internal` separates engine defects (violated invariants, invalid finding prose, invalid limits, hash failures within validated limits) from input failures (an unreadable root, a change during the run, an unknown package, a full finding collector, exhausted storage).

**Document.** `Inspection::document()` returns `frameshift.inspection` version 1, described by [frameshift.inspection.schema.json](../schemas/frameshift.inspection.schema.json) and listed in `D-075`. `src/document.rs` builds a value tree with members in a fixed order and prints it with two-space indentation, LF line ends, and a final LF; strings escape `"`, `\`, and C0 controls as RFC 8259 requires, and DEL, the C1 controls, U+2028, U+2029, and the twelve Bidi_Control characters (U+061C, U+200E, U+200F, U+202A to U+202E, U+2066 to U+2069) as `\u` escapes, so a document shown in a terminal cannot start an escape sequence or reorder text (`D-076`), and keep other characters as UTF-8; numbers are nonnegative integers. Report spellings come from `as_str` methods on the types (`Verdict`, `ConfigLoad`, `Router`, `DetectionSource`, `IgnoredReason`, `WorkspaceSemantics`, `IgnoreReason`) and `Stop::limit_name`. Findings carry their report-local ID, code, severity, location (`project`, `file` with a path, or `text` with one-based positions and byte offsets), optional unit, feature, and rule, and their prose.

**Command.** `frameshift inspect [<source>] [--package <dir>] [--format text|json]` ([CLI.md](CLI.md), section 4.1). `src/args.rs` lets only the source operand be a non-Unicode native path; any other non-Unicode argument remains a usage error, which takes precedence as before. `--package .` selects the root; any other value must be a canonical `SourcePath`. The text renderer prints files, package manager, lockfile, workspace, and per package its framework, configuration file, and routers, then every finding on one line with severity, code, location, and message. Source-derived strings (paths, names, finding prose) are escaped: backslashes, control characters, U+2028, U+2029, and the bidirectional formatting characters U+061C, U+200E, U+200F, U+202A to U+202E, and U+2066 to U+2069 become `\u{..}` (U+061C since `D-076`). The source path itself is never printed. JSON output is the engine's document; the CLI's own `frameshift.version` and `frameshift.error` documents escape the same characters. Results exit with 0, usage errors with 2, input failures with 3, and engine defects with 70; with JSON requested, failures also print a `frameshift.error` document with that code.

**Fixtures and tests.** Seven fixtures of kind `inspect` below `fixtures/discovery/` commit route and configuration files; their manifests and lockfiles that name the real `next` package are created at test time from `createdAtTestTime` (`Q-014`). `inspect-next-app`, `inspect-next-workspace`, and `inspect-next-legacy` cover the `D-075` facts. The adversarial cases of `D-076` cover hostile names (`inspect-hostile-names`: terminal escape sequences, U+009B, a fake output line, U+2028, a backslash, U+202E, and U+061C in package and directory names, created at test time), links as structure (`inspect-links`: a linked configuration file, `app` directory, member manifest, `src` directory, and member directory, a dangling `app` link, and a lockfile link that leaves the root), resource exhaustion within the default limits (`inspect-resource-exhaustion`: a member manifest and a lockfile nested 300 levels deep and an ignored `pnpm-workspace.yaml` nested beyond the YAML depth limit), and contradictory evidence (`inspect-conflicting-evidence`: three configuration files, `app` beside `src/app`, `src/pages` beside `app`, duplicate and repeated `next` declarations, a lockfile of another package manager, case-dependent patterns, duplicate member names, and a directory named `next.config.js`). Both harnesses create symbolic links at test time; a host that cannot create them (Windows without the privilege) skips `inspect-links`, whose goldens were produced on Linux. `crates/frameshift-engine/tests/inspect_fixtures.rs` compares the document, with the engine version as `@ENGINE_VERSION@`, with `expected/inspection.json` and checks that repeated inspections are identical; `crates/frameshift-cli/tests/inspect.rs` compares the CLI text with `expected/inspection.txt` and the JSON output with the engine golden, and covers package selection, the current directory as default source, exit code 3 with error documents that never name the source path, usage errors, and a non-Unicode source path. Five facade tests in `crates/frameshift-engine/tests/inspect.rs` cover a pnpm project with an ignored npm lockfile and built-in ignores, the three package scopes with a selection error and a name with a control character, a project without a framework, a stopping limit, and the classification of input failures and invalid limits. Unit tests cover the document writer's escapes and layout, argument parsing, and terminal escaping. Goldens change only through:

```text
cargo test -p frameshift-engine --offline --locked --test inspect_fixtures -- --ignored update_inspect_goldens
cargo test -p frameshift-cli --offline --locked --test inspect -- --ignored update_inspect_text_goldens
```

The packaging script inspects `fixtures/discovery/workspace-npm/source` with each packaged binary and checks the document kind and members (section 13).

**Validation (2026-10-21).** On Windows x86_64 with Rust 1.98.0, the workspace builds offline with the locked graph, and the debug and release workspace suites each pass 451 tests with zero failures; the 13 ignored tests are the six golden updaters and seven external runners. Formatting, all-target Clippy with warnings denied, and the repository policy checker with its 13 regression cases pass. `inspect --format json` on the source of every one of the 29 fixtures below `fixtures/discovery/` exits with 0, and every document validates against the schema with PowerShell 7.6 `Test-Json`, including findings with project, file, and text locations; documents with an unknown router or location scope are rejected. Linux test runs in WSL were not repeated for this increment; native six-platform CI covers them.

**Measurements (2026-10-21).** On the development desktop (section 6) with a warm filesystem cache, a release build inspected the projects of the approved real-world sample (`D-043`, outside the repository) with default limits; times are the minimum of five runs of the whole process with JSON output, and peak working sets the largest of three runs polled during execution. The largest single project, 6,071 admitted files, took 131 ms with a peak of 19.3 MB; the 72-package pnpm workspace with 2,677 files took 136 ms; the whole sample as one root, 22,921 files, took 528 ms with 16.5 MB. This is within the budget of section 4 of [ENGINEERING.md](ENGINEERING.md) (5,000 files under 0.5 s and 200 MB) on this machine; reference-hardware measurements remain M11 work. The detections match section 31.

**Validation (`D-076`, 2026-10-21).** On Windows x86_64 with Rust 1.98.0, the debug workspace suite passes 452 tests with zero failures and 13 ignored tests, with formatting and all-target Clippy with warnings denied; `inspect-links` is skipped there. Static musl test binaries in WSL pass the inspect fixtures, including `inspect-links`, the discovery fixtures, and the seven Next.js facade tests, including the Unix-only link test. The CLI's process tests do not run in WSL, because their binary paths are embedded Windows paths; native six-platform CI runs them. The existing inspect goldens are unchanged except for the corrected `FS1043` explanation in `inspect-next-legacy`.

**Limitations.** Language, styling, static export, and file accounting by unit are not reported; `migrationPaths` stays empty until a ruleset ships. A source path that starts with `-` must be written with a `./` prefix. `--report` and the other planned global options are not implemented for `inspect`.

## 33. CSS tokenizer (`D-077`)

Implemented under the owner-confirmed `D-077` in `crates/frameshift-syntax/src/css.rs`, exposed through `frameshift_engine::syntax`. No dependency is added. It is the first M3 increment; the generic structural parser in section 34 now consumes the tokens. The Sass parser and its completed syntax-conformance evidence are in section 35 (`D-090`).

**Specification.** The tokenizer of CSS Syntax Level 3, Editor's Draft at csswg-drafts revision `c410ee5e4d2afe6d8b2e8ce53723b8d1eb3b5b67` (2026-10-20): "consume a token" with its optional *unicode ranges allowed* flag, the comment, numeric, ident-like, string, URL, escape, ident sequence, number, unicode-range, and bad URL remnant algorithms, and the draft's non-ASCII ident code points.

**API.** `tokenize_css(source, CssLimits)` returns `CssTokens`: whether a leading UTF-8 byte order mark was skipped, the tokens in source order without EOF, the comments as spans including `/*` and `*/`, and the parse errors. `scan_css_token(source, offset, CssTokenContext)` consumes comments and one token at a parser-selected offset and returns them with their errors, or no token at EOF; it does not skip a byte order mark. `CssTokenContext::unicode_ranges_allowed` is false by default, as the draft's entry points use it. `css_token_value` returns the decoded name of an ident, function, at-keyword, or hash token, the content of a string, bad string up to its newline, or URL, the code point of a delimiter, and nothing for other tokens; `css_dimension_unit` decodes a dimension's unit. Both rescan the token at its offset and fail with `CssError::ForeignToken` for a token this source does not produce there. A name without escapes or NUL is borrowed from the source. Since `D-078`, value validation checks unicode-range tokens with their required scanning context and returns no decoded value for them.

**Tokens.** `CssTokenKind` has the draft's tokens: ident, function (whose span includes `(`, and for a quoted `url(` argument every whitespace code point the draft consumes before the quote), at-keyword, hash with its `Id` or `Unrestricted` flag, string, bad string, URL, bad URL, delimiter with its code point, number, percentage, and dimension with a `CssNumber` (type flag, sign, and the exact text including sign and exponent; a percentage excludes `%`, a dimension has a separate unit span), unicode range with its inclusive bounds (only with the context flag), whitespace, CDO, CDC, colon, semicolon, comma, and the six brackets. No floating-point value is computed.

**Preprocessing.** The original text is never copied: a reader maps CR LF, CR, and FF to one LF and NUL to U+FFFD while scanning, so offsets stay original byte offsets. A NUL is therefore an ident code point, and an escape's single trailing whitespace may be a CR LF pair. Surrogates cannot occur in UTF-8 text.

**Errors.** Tokenization never fails on its input. `CssParseErrorKind` records each parse error of the tokenizer algorithms with the offset of the code point where it occurs, or the text length at EOF: an unterminated comment, string, or URL, a newline in a string, a quote, `(`, or non-printable code point in an unquoted URL, an invalid escape in an unquoted URL, a backslash before a newline at a token start, and an escape at EOF. The tokens are those of the draft's recovery. Partition: tokens, comments, and the byte order mark cover the text exactly once, and every span is non-empty.

**Limits.** `css.max-tokens` (default 4,194,304) bounds tokens and comments of one `tokenize_css` call; one more fails with `CssError::TokenLimit` and no result. Scanning is iterative, and look-ahead never exceeds three code points.

**Tests.** Ten facade tests in `crates/frameshift-engine/tests/css.rs` cover rules, declarations, and comments; preprocessing; escapes and their replacement rules; strings, bad strings, and unterminated strings; URLs and quoted `url(` functions; numbers, percentages, and dimensions; hashes, at-keywords, CDO, and CDC; unicode ranges with and without the context; the byte order mark, comments, and the inclusive limit; and foreign tokens, offsets, and rescanning at every token. Every test checks the partition. The `css` fuzz target is listed in section 7. The opt-in conformance runner compares tokenization, grouped into component values, with `component_value_list.json` of css-parsing-tests:

```text
FRAMESHIFT_CSS_PARSING_TESTS=<css-parsing-tests checkout> cargo test -p frameshift-engine --offline --locked --test css external_css_parsing_tests -- --ignored --nocapture
```

The suite follows the 2021 Candidate Recommendation, so the runner tokenizes with unicode ranges allowed, reads its Level 2 attribute-match and column tokens as two delimiters, and maps its `eof-in-string` and `eof-in-url` markers to the recorded parse errors.

**Evidence (2026-10-21).** css-parsing-tests at commit `203ce36bffd617db7f118c551e32794561fb273d`, kept outside the repository: all 50 cases of `component_value_list.json` agree except one, where the suite counts U+0080 and U+0081 as ident code points and the draft does not; the runner expects exactly that difference. A development program outside the repository compared the token streams, including comment and whitespace spans, decoded values, and number flags at single precision, with cssparser 0.37.0, the tokenizer that Lightning CSS 1.0.0-alpha.72 uses, nesting its blocks to obtain a flat stream. On the 617 CSS files of the approved real-world sample (`D-043`, 1,724,844 bytes), all token streams are identical. On 50,000 generated fictional inputs of up to 24 pieces each, 41,895 are identical; every first difference falls into three classes: code points from U+0080 that cssparser, following the older rule, counts as ident code points and the draft does not (8,059 inputs); `-` before a backslash and a newline, which cssparser reads as an ident and the draft as a delimiter, because the backslash starts no valid escape (34); and the one whitespace code point that the draft's function token for `url(` with a quoted argument consumes and cssparser leaves to the next token (12). Without the code points of the first class, 49,928 of 50,000 generated inputs are identical, and the other 72 fall into the second and third classes. A coverage-instrumented run of the `css` target on the development desktop executed 3,441,319 inputs in 300 wall-clock seconds, reached 5,614 coverage features with 1,475 corpus inputs, and found nothing; this is a smoke run, not a budget run.

**Validation (2026-10-21).** On Windows x86_64 with Rust 1.98.0, the debug and release workspace suites each pass 462 tests with zero failures; the 14 ignored tests are the six golden updaters and eight external runners. Formatting, all-target Clippy with warnings denied, and the repository policy checker pass. The fuzzer's unit tests run every target's seeds, including the nine fictional `css` seeds.

**Limitations.** Generic parsing and nesting are implemented in section 34. The Sass parser begins in section 35, with full conformance still required; nothing interprets imports, `url()` references, CSS Modules, or Tailwind directives yet. Number values are not converted. The input is decoded `SourceText`; the draft's byte-stream decoding with `@charset` and other encodings is not implemented, and text that is not UTF-8 stays unanalyzed (`FS2002`).

## 34. Generic CSS parser (`D-078`)

Implemented in `crates/frameshift-syntax/src/css_parser.rs`, exposed through `frameshift_engine::syntax`, with no new dependency. `parse_css(source, CssLimits, CssParserLimits)` parses a whole UTF-8 stylesheet using the CSS Syntax draft pinned in section 33. It applies the draft's generic declaration-versus-rule test, without a catalog of recognized properties or valid selectors. Unknown at-rules remain represented. Imports, URLs, CSS Modules, and Tailwind are not yet interpreted. The Sass parser and its syntax-conformance evidence are described in section 35 (`D-079`, `D-090`).

**Representation.** `CssDocument` retains default-context `CssTokens` and their exact partition, a flat component arena with top-level `values()`, a flat syntax arena with top-level `rules()`, and diagnostics. Component and syntax indices are document-local; `component` and `node` return `None` for an out-of-range index. Components retain the opening token, source extent, ordered child indices, and optional closing token. Simple blocks and functions are grouped iteratively. Blocks closed by EOF have no closer and extend to EOF; CSS automatic closure alone is not a parse error. Arena entries own indices rather than nested objects, so destruction is not recursive.

**Structure.** `CssRule` distinguishes an at-rule by its name token, keeps prelude components and an optional body block, and lists declarations and nested rules in source order. Consecutive declarations represent the draft's declaration lists; later consumers decide their CSSOM interpretation. `CssDeclaration` keeps its name token, value components, and priority. Names are decoded for comparison without changing source spelling. Trailing `!important` is ASCII case-insensitive, may contain comments, and is removed from the value with surrounding whitespace. Custom properties retain arbitrary block values; other generic declarations accept a top-level curly block only as the whole value. Mixed curly values trigger rule interpretation, with early exits to avoid quadratic rescanning of repeated pseudo-class rules. A `unicode-range` declaration receives alternate components tokenized with unicode ranges enabled, as the draft requires; original tokens and components remain available. Declaration spans exclude the semicolon but include the original value and priority; at-rule spans include a terminating semicolon when present.

**Errors and recovery.** Tokenizer errors, unmatched closing delimiters, malformed generic rules, and top-level exclamation marks outside declaration priority remain diagnostics in stable source order. Recovery consumes whole components until a semicolon, enclosing block boundary, or EOF. Discarded syntax stays available through the original components. `has_errors()` prohibits transformation of the entire file under `D-034`; a clean generic tree establishes neither semantic CSS validity nor migration support. Selector, property-value, and individual at-rule grammars belong to later analysis. Optional JavaScript diagnostic recovery is implemented separately in section 36.

**Limits.** `CssParserLimits` adds `css.parser.max-nodes` (4,194,304 combined component and syntax entries, including alternate unicode-range components), `css.parser.max-depth` (128, also the implementation ceiling), `css.parser.max-diagnostics` (256 tokenizer and structural diagnostics together), and `css.parser.max-work` (16,777,216 token/component visits during grouping, disambiguation, recovery, and descriptor rescanning). Tokenization remains bounded separately by `CssLimits`. Structural rule traversal is recursive within the checked component depth. Exceeding a bound returns `CssParserError` without a partial document. The engine limit set now has 44 entries.

**Tests.** Twelve facade tests in `crates/frameshift-engine/tests/css_parser.rs` cover declarations around nested rules and at-rules, generic disambiguation, custom properties, priority decoding, functions and blocks, EOF closure, unicode-range rescanning, recovery with later siblings, lexical errors, unmatched closers, Unicode and CRLF spans, zero and inclusive bounds, the depth ceiling with 128 nested rules, and 5,000 ambiguous rules within a linear work allowance. The `css` fuzz target also checks repetition, component and syntax span containment and ordering, diagnostic coordinates and ordering, and inclusive node and diagnostic limits, with fictional nesting and recovery seeds.

**External conformance.** `external_css_stylesheets` in `crates/frameshift-engine/tests/css.rs` compares rule kinds, names, preludes, and original block components against all 16 cases of `stylesheet.json` in css-parsing-tests at revision `203ce36bffd617db7f118c551e32794561fb273d`, outside the repository. The older suite does not check modern declaration/nesting interpretation; facade tests cover those boundaries. Set `FRAMESHIFT_CSS_PARSING_TESTS` as in section 33 and run:

```text
cargo test -p frameshift-engine --offline --locked --test css external_css_stylesheets -- --ignored --nocapture
```

On Windows x86_64, all 16 stylesheet cases pass. The 50 existing component-value cases still pass with the single documented draft difference.

**Rescanning regression.** The first instrumented parser run found a component-containment violation after 180,404 executions. It reduced to `{unicode-range:U+4URL(>')[');`: enabling unicode ranges changes later URL/string token boundaries, so rescanning against the whole stylesheet could read past the original declaration value. The private scanner now sees EOF at the value segment boundary while preserving original offsets, and rescanning diagnostics are retained. The minimized case is a facade regression and a permanent fictional fuzz seed; the original finding passes replay. This does not change the public whole-source token scanner.

**Validation (2026-10-21).** On Windows x86_64 with Rust 1.98.0, the final debug and release workspace suites each pass 474 tests with zero failures; 15 tests are deliberately ignored golden updaters or external runners. The offline locked workspace build, formatting, all-target Clippy with warnings denied, repository policy checker and its 13 regression cases, E2 goldens, all 3,000 generated corpus contracts, documentation file links, and the absence of em dashes pass. Both external CSS runners also pass in release mode: 16 stylesheet cases and 50 component-value cases with the single documented draft difference. No live CI or additional native platform run was performed for this increment.

After the descriptor-boundary fix, an instrumented 180-second smoke run completed 1,219,093 executions without findings. On the final implementation, with additional decoding checks for every component of a diagnostic-free document, a separate instrumented 120-second run completed 902,883 executions, reached 8,457 coverage features, and saved 2,108 corpus inputs without findings. These are wall-clock smoke runs, not the CPU-hour budget of `D-044`. Unicode-range token validation now uses its required scanner context, returning no decoded value instead of falsely rejecting a genuine range as foreign; the facade test and fuzz contract cover this.

## 35. SCSS and indented Sass parser (`D-079` to `D-090`)

Implemented in `crates/frameshift-syntax/src/sass.rs`, `src/sass/expression.rs`, `src/sass/statement.rs`, `src/sass/selector.rs`, `src/sass/module.rs`, `src/sass/module_url.rs`, `src/sass/import.rs`, `src/sass/condition.rs`, `src/sass/extend.rs`, `src/sass/at_root.rs`, and `src/sass/if_expression.rs`, exposed through `frameshift_engine::syntax`. The complete-parser scope requested by the owner has syntax-conformance evidence against the pinned normal parser under `D-090`. Evaluation-dependent and deliberately raw grammars retain evidence; M3 remains in progress. No runtime dependency is added.

**Reference and boundaries.** Dart Sass 1.105.2 at commit `00a0d7968f4a6d8ed044da35e3b04877d8b3e2b4`, and the Sass specification at revision `57c81dd74eb5c301d7d81cd77cd019cc4d9bac1e`, kept outside the repository. The parser reads only immutable UTF-8 `SourceText`: no filesystem access, import resolution, expression evaluation, or external process. In particular, parsing `@use` or `@import` never opens its URL.

**API and representation.** `parse_sass(source, SassSyntax, SassLimits)` selects `Scss` or `Indented` explicitly. Its `SassDocument` borrows the original source and owns a flat `SassNode` arena, document-local indices, ordered top-level statements, and source-ordered comments. Each node has a kind, its exact original span, and immediate children in source order. Strings and interpolated text keep static content in the original source and retain interpolation expressions as children. Loud comments retain their own interpolation roots; silent comments do not interpret their contents. All nodes are reachable from statements or comment interpolations. Arena ownership and destruction are iterative. Numbers retain their spelling without floating-point conversion, and declarations retain flags and names without rewriting underscores or escapes.

**Implemented grammar.** Variable declarations and namespaced references; ordinary and nested property declarations; custom-property raw values; nested style rules with structured selectors (`D-081`, below); Sass module, callable, content, control-flow, diagnostic, and extension directives; `@media` and `@supports` conditions and import modifiers (`D-083`, below); `@extend` targets and `@at-root` queries (`D-084`, below); interpolation in names, strings, raw CSS, and loud comments. SassScript includes unary and binary precedence, whitespace and comma lists, bracketed lists, parentheses and maps, exact numeric spellings and units, hexadecimal colors, Unicode ranges, strings, booleans, null, parent-selector expressions, namespaced calls, keyword arguments, rest arguments, parameters and defaults. Callable duplicate checks decode escapes and fold underscores as Sass does. Syntactic context checks reject misplaced returns, content, module directives, duplicate else branches, private namespaced expression accesses, and nested callable declarations. CSS function argument `=` is distinct from Sass equality and is not accepted in mixin arguments.

**Surface syntaxes.** SCSS uses braces and semicolons. Indented Sass reads original newline and indentation prefixes without rewriting the source. Dedents must match an enclosing level; a document cannot mix tabs and spaces. Parenthesized and argument-list expressions allow newlines. Comma-continued selectors, optional trailing statement semicolons, empty indented blocks, and indentation-delimited silent and loud comments are supported. Byte order marks, Unicode names, CRLF, and escaped string newlines retain original offsets.

**Legacy indented shorthand (`D-080`).** `=name` defines a mixin and `+name` includes one, using the same parameters, arguments, namespaces, `using` parameters, content blocks, syntactic contexts, and limits as their long forms. A plus followed by whitespace or interpolation remains selector syntax. These forms use the existing `AtRule(Mixin)` and `AtRule(Include)` kinds, with the original marker in the statement span and no directive-name child; the first child is the callable name. Long-form directives retain their directive-name child. A leading backslash forces a style rule in indented syntax: the rule span includes that backslash, while its selector child's span starts after it. SCSS does not enable these forms. The pinned Dart Sass version no longer treats `:property value` as a legacy property declaration; any retained selector still carries unchecked-selector evidence.

**Module rules (`D-082`).** `src/sass/module.rs` parses `@use` and `@forward`, with URI syntax isolated in `src/sass/module_url.rs`. Their URL child is a quoted `String` without interpolation children: `#{...}` in a module URL is literal text. Validation checks URI schemes, authorities, ports, bracketed IP literals (IPv6 through the Rust standard library), data URLs, and the default namespace derived from the percent-decoded last path segment, before its first dot and after removing one leading underscore. Explicit `as` clauses bypass default-namespace derivation, but never URL validation. Names and URLs preserve source spelling; no URL is opened and no module is resolved. Duplicate namespaces and availability or visibility of configured members require semantic analysis, not parsing.

A module rule retains its directive-name and URL children. Explicit `@use as` clauses add `ModuleNamespace { global }` with zero children for `as *`, or one identifier child. Forwarding prefixes add `ForwardPrefix` with one identifier child; `ForwardVisibility { hidden }` distinguishes `hide` from `show` and retains the source-ordered variable and identifier children, including duplicates. Clause spans start at their keyword and end at the marker or last member. Configuration remains an `Arguments` node of `Argument` children, with `!default` accepted only for forwarding. Escaped and case-insensitive clause keywords follow the pinned parser; `!default` remains case-sensitive. Module directives remain root-only and precede ordinary rules; variables and root `@charset` rules leave that prefix open. Charset rules retain their source as CSS directives. The pinned compiler intercepts the literal root prefix `@charset` without an identifier boundary: root names such as `@charsetfoo` fail while expecting a string, whereas the same nested name follows generic at-rule parsing. This intentional reference behavior is covered in both syntax modes. Literal URL strings follow the pinned plain-string reader, including rejection of an escaped CRLF pair. URL/namespace passes charge eight work units per decoded URL byte in addition to reader visits. The existing node, depth, and work limits apply; no limit or dependency is added. `SassUncheckedKind::ModuleUrl` is removed because module-rule syntax is now checked. This does not change the whole-file transformation prohibition.

**Import items (continuation of `D-079`).** `src/sass/import.rs` replaces the raw `@import` header with source-ordered `Import { css }` items after the directive-name child. Each item has its URL child and, if present, one `ImportModifiers` child. Quoted paths are literal strings without interpolation, including quoted CSS paths such as `"theme-#{$name}.css"`; indented Sass also accepts literal unquoted paths through comma or line end, including spaces and an empty path. A `url()` URL is a `Call`: its identifier is followed by raw `InterpolatedText` with parsed interpolation, or by `Arguments` when SassScript fallback is necessary. Speculation discards its nodes and comments without restoring work. No URL is opened or resolved.

Classification follows Dart Sass 1.105.2: `url()` and paths with modifiers are CSS imports; otherwise a literal path is CSS only when it has at least five UTF-16 code units and ends in lowercase `.css`, starts with `//`, or starts with lowercase `http://` or `https://`. Other paths are Sass imports, whose URI syntax is validated with the module URL reader. Absolute Windows drive paths use the reference's file-URI interpretation; UNC authorities still require validation. Validation charges eight work units per decoded URL byte. Sass imports are rejected in mixins and control blocks, while CSS imports remain permitted there; the existing function and nested-property restrictions apply to both kinds. Include content alone permits Sass imports.

Modifier boundaries preserve Sass's comma ambiguity: a comma after a bare modifier identifier introduces a media-query list, while a comma after a modifier function can introduce another import item. Modifiers are structured with the reference's modifier grammar under `D-083` (below) and carry no unchecked evidence. Import items establish structure, URL syntax, classification, and context restrictions, not complete import conformance or transformation eligibility.

**Media queries and supports conditions (`D-083`).** `src/sass/condition.rs` parses `@media` and `@supports` headers and import modifiers with the parse-time grammars of Dart Sass 1.105.2. `@media` and `@supports` are `AtRule(Media)` and `AtRule(Supports)`, rejected in functions and nested properties like other CSS at-rules, with a block that inherits the enclosing statement context. A `MediaQueryList` holds comma-separated `MediaQuery` nodes; a query holds modifier and type names, `ConditionOperator` keywords (`and`, `or`, `not`, as spelled), and `MediaInParens` or `Interpolation` conditions. A `MediaInParens` holds nested conditions with keywords, `not` and its operand, or a feature: one SassScript expression, optionally followed by `MediaRelation` (`:`, `=`, `<`, `<=`, `>`, `>=`) and expression pairs, at most two in a range that repeats its direction. Feature operands before a relation end at a top-level `<`, `>`, or single `=`; parenthesized, argument, and interpolated expressions inside them do not. Newlines in indented Sass are consumed only where the reference consumes them, which is inside parentheses except between `and` or `or` sequences.

Supports conditions are `SupportsNegation`, `SupportsOperation` (one repeated `and` or `or`), `SupportsDeclaration`, `SupportsFunction`, `SupportsAnything`, or a sole `Interpolation`; a parenthesized condition's span includes its parentheses. A declaration is tried first as `Expression ":" Expression`. After a grammar failure, the parser restores its position, arena, comments, and flags, keeps its work charges, and reads an interpolated name followed either by operators (for a sole interpolation) or by raw text up to a top-level colon; a colon there reports the original failure. Custom property values, function arguments, and fallback text use the reference's raw declaration-value reader: balanced brackets, strings, escapes, interpolation, raw `url()` contents, silent comments, and loud comments whose contents are not interpolated. Inside `@import`, `ImportModifiers` holds bare names, `ImportFunction` nodes (a `supports()` condition, which also admits a bare declaration or function, or raw arguments for other names), and a final `MediaQueryList`.

During evaluation, Dart Sass parses the evaluated media query text again with a stricter grammar, but serializes supports conditions and import modifiers without a second parse. A `@media` query list therefore carries `SassUncheckedKind::MediaQuery` evidence exactly when it contains interpolation, a variable, a call or special function, a quoted string, a parent selector, `null`, or a map; the walk charges one work unit per node. Supports conditions and import modifiers, including their media query lists, carry no evidence; `ImportPrelude` is removed. Raw brackets and parenthesized conditions share the existing depth limit. Media query merging during evaluation is not modeled.

**Extension targets and `@at-root` queries (`D-084`).** `src/sass/extend.rs` reads an `@extend` header as Dart Sass 1.105.2 does: whitespace and comments before the target, including line ends in indented syntax, then raw text up to `!`, `;`, `{`, `}`, or an indented line end outside brackets, with raw escapes, whole strings and interpolated identifiers, raw `url()` contents, uninterpolated loud comments, and an error for an unbalanced closing bracket. An empty target is accepted. A following `!optional` becomes a `Flag` child; each letter may be escaped and its case is ignored, and an identifier character after it fails. The target span excludes trailing whitespace unless the last character before it is a backslash, as evaluation trims the text. An interpolation-free, non-empty target is then parsed within that extent with the evaluation selector grammar of `D-081`, where `&`, including inside selector pseudo-classes, is rejected. When the whole extent parses and every complex selector is one compound selector of one simple selector, the target is a `SelectorList` without evidence. Otherwise the parser restores the raw arena and comments, keeps its work charges, and retains the raw `InterpolatedText` with `Selector` evidence; such targets include interpolation, descendant or explicit combinators, compound selectors, `&`, `url()`, strings, and the empty target, all of which evaluation rejects or re-parses.
