`src/sass/at_root.rs` parses a query with the reference's parse-time grammar: `(`, one SassScript expression, an optional colon and second expression, and `)`, with line ends allowed inside the parentheses in both syntaxes. The result is an `AtRootQuery` node, including its parentheses, with one or two expression children. Evaluation joins the evaluated texts as `(kind: rules)` and requires `with` or `without`, a colon, and identifiers. A query therefore carries no evidence only when its first child is an unquoted identifier without interpolation whose decoded text is `with` or `without` in any case, and its second is such an identifier or a space-separated list of them; hash words and `%` are not identifiers. Every other query carries `SassUncheckedKind::AtRootPrelude` evidence on the query node. The enclosing keyframes context still becomes evaluation-dependent for children of any query. `SassUncheckedKind::Selector` is no longer reported for every `@extend` rule.

**Modern CSS-style `if()` (`D-085`).** `src/sass/if_expression.rs` parses modern branches and conditions against the same pinned reference. `IfExpression` keeps its name and source-ordered `IfBranch { otherwise }` children; each branch keeps its condition (or the `else` identifier) and its SassScript value. Conditions have dedicated `IfConditionSass`, `IfConditionFunction`, `IfConditionParenthesized`, `IfConditionNegation`, `IfConditionOperation`, and `IfConditionRaw` kinds. Operations retain source-spelled `ConditionOperator` children. Function conditions preserve their name and raw interpolated arguments. Repeated `else` branches and a trailing semicolon follow the reference grammar. An empty modern expression is rejected; lowercase legacy `if()` remains a call.

Only the decoded lowercase unnamespaced `if` tries legacy arguments first. Failed speculation restores syntax, comments, and grammar flags, but keeps work charges and propagates limit errors. Case variants use the modern grammar directly; namespaced calls remain ordinary calls. Arbitrary substitution follows the reference's `if`, `var`, `attr`, custom-function, and interpolation rules, including rejection of raw condition sequences containing `sass()`. Inline loud trivia and raw groups preserve interpolation markers as text, correcting their former attempt to parse discarded comment contents; retained standalone loud comments still parse interpolation. All existing Sass limits apply. A media query containing a modern expression carries `MediaQuery` evidence. Conditions are syntax, not evaluated results, and no Sass result authorizes transformation.

**Evaluation-dependent and deliberately raw grammar.** `unchecked_syntax()` explicitly identifies selectors whose grammar depends on evaluation (`D-081`: interpolation, include-site context, or raw text), `@extend` targets that are not established lists of simple selectors (`D-084`), `@media` query lists whose evaluated text depends on values (`D-083`), CSS or unknown directives, `@at-root` queries that are not literal `with` or `without` queries with plain identifiers (`D-084`), and raw special-function contents. This iterator is in arena construction order. Those regions follow the normal Sass parse-time grammar, which intentionally defers evaluated selectors, queries, and CSS semantics. They can therefore include input that Dart Sass rejects during evaluation. An empty evidence iterator does not establish evaluation validity, resolved imports, or migration support. `D-090` establishes the syntax-conformance scope of `D-079`; future analysis and transformation rules must still handle every relevant evidence entry and semantic precondition.

**Raw expression URLs (`D-087`).** Expression parsing now tries Dart Sass's raw URL lexical grammar before SassScript arguments. Names are decoded without underscore folding; case-insensitive `url` and names whose vendor prefix is removed to `url` select the grammar, including escaped names. Namespaced and interpolated names remain ordinary calls. The shared `raw_url_contents` reader in `src/sass/condition.rs` serves expression, import, and raw declaration contexts. It accepts leading/trailing whitespace and raw URL characters, including literal brackets, braces, semicolons, and comment markers; unescaped whitespace between body characters or a quoted argument falls back to SassScript. Interpolation is parsed, and malformed interpolation or a limit error propagates immediately. Failed raw lookahead restores syntax, comments, depth, and grammar flags while retaining its work charges. Out-of-range escaped code points fail in import URLs as well.

A raw expression URL is an unquoted `String` spanning its original name and parentheses, with interpolation children only, and no `SpecialFunction` evidence. Literal comment markers inside its body are not comments. A fallback is a `Call` with a name and `Arguments`; import URLs preserve their existing `Call` representation with either `InterpolatedText` or `Arguments` as the second child. This establishes URL syntax and classification, not URI validity, evaluation, or transformation eligibility. Other special functions remain explicitly unchecked.

**Raw special functions (`D-088`).** `src/sass/expression.rs` dispatches on decoded case-insensitive names without underscore folding. Unprefixed `type()`, vendor-prefixed `calc()`, and prefixed or unprefixed `expression()` and `element()` use the shared raw declaration-value grammar. `progid:` reads an ASCII-letter/dot path, possibly empty, followed immediately by parentheses; `progid()` is an ordinary call. Namespaced or interpolated names never select special syntax. A `SpecialFunction` has an `Identifier` child spanning the whole callable name (including a `progid:` path) and an `InterpolatedText` child spanning its parentheses. Its contents retain quoted-string and interpolation children and source-ordered comments, accept empty values and nested semicolons, reject top-level semicolons and mismatched brackets, recognize raw URLs, and honor indented line boundaries. These nodes still carry `SpecialFunction` unchecked evidence; syntax does not establish CSS semantics or transformation eligibility. The outer function parentheses charge one shared depth level, including for an empty value, and release it before sibling expressions; nested raw groups charge additional levels. Existing shared limits apply.

The opt-in `external_special_function_classification_oracle` in `tests/sass/special_function.rs` shares the URL classification runner and checks 2,240 fictional cases in both syntaxes against pinned `sass-parser` 0.4.58 and Sass 1.105.2. All agree on acceptance and string-versus-call classification. Facade tests check dispatch, exact spans, comments, interpolation, invalid boundaries, repeated trees, and inclusive node/work limits; the Sass fuzzer checks the two-child representation and evidence.

**Numeric unit boundaries (`D-089`).** Number parsing follows Dart Sass 1.105.2's unit-specific identifier reader. A literal `--` prefix is not a unit; a literal hyphen followed by an ASCII digit or decimal point terminates a unit. Other identifier hyphens and CSS escapes remain part of the dimension. Thus `1px-2px` and `1px-.5rem` produce `Binary(Subtract)` with exact `Number` operands, `1px-name` remains one `Number`, and `1--unit` is a space-separated list of a number and an unquoted string. Identifier-starting hyphens in expression context are not subtraction operators. The general identifier grammar is unchanged. No values are computed; existing node, depth, and work bounds apply.

Three facade tests in `tests/sass/number.rs` check operand structure and exact spans, literal and escaped unit boundaries, Unicode/BOM/CRLF, both syntaxes, repeated trees/errors, and inclusive shared limits. The opt-in `external_number_span_oracle` compares acceptance and exact numeric source spelling and UTF-8 spans for 864 fictional cases with pinned `sass-parser` 0.4.58 and Sass 1.105.2, without evaluation. Those cases also extend the parse-only oracle. Run it with the same `FRAMESHIFT_SASS_PARSER_MODULE` as the other Sass oracles. The `D-090` closure extends these cases to signed numeric spans.

**Plain CSS functions and callable names (`D-086`).** Lowercase `@function` dispatches on a literal `--` prefix before Sass callable-context checks, as the pinned reference does. Such a rule is `AtRule(Css)` with its source-spelled name, raw `InterpolatedText` header, ordinary body children, and `CssDirective` evidence. CSS function signatures remain unchecked. Case variants such as `@FUNCTION` already use the unknown CSS rule path and also enable raw result values. A literal `@mixin --name` is a syntax/context error in the reference and is rejected with `InvalidContext`; underscore-prefixed and escaped-hyphen callable names retain the Sass grammar. Sass function headers permit newlines before their name and parameter list in both syntaxes.

Within a plain CSS function, case-insensitive, interpolation-free `result:` names select raw declaration values, including parsed interpolation, quoted strings, balanced groups, uninterpreted loud comments, and literal `//` text. The value preserves whitespace after the colon; a name with a glued loud comment, such as `result/**/:`, remains an ordinary SassScript declaration. The context is inherited by nested blocks and does not leak to following siblings. An immediate statement boundary produces an empty raw value; in SCSS an immediate opening brace is also a boundary and the required separator rejects it, while whitespace before that brace allows a raw group. An indented value consisting only of whitespace before its line end is rejected. All reads, speculation, groups, comments, and syntax nodes use the existing shared limits.

**Sass syntax closure (`D-090`).** Remaining parse-time gaps are closed against Dart Sass 1.105.2's normal parser. `var(--tone,)` has a second `Argument` containing an unquoted empty `String`, both with zero-width spans at the closing parenthesis after any trivia. Dispatch uses the decoded, case-insensitive unnamespaced literal name, without vendor stripping; namespaced, interpolated, named, rest, and ordinary trailing-comma calls do not acquire a fallback. Signed numeric literals include their sign in `Number`; unary slash has `SassUnaryOperator::Divide`. Trailing `%` becomes a space-list string, `1 +2` is addition, and comma-list operator boundaries and signed-number rest boundaries follow the reference. A comma list remains a list when a comment follows its final comma. At the start of an expression or an operator operand, signed numeric literals include their sign in `Number`; after a top-level comma, a plus selects a unary operation instead. Hyphen-prefixed interpolation is a string, not a minus operand. Unary `not` uses the decoded, case-sensitive name. Binary `and` and `or` follow the reference expression loop: a literal lower-case first letter, followed by case-insensitive and escape-aware identifier scanning. Lookahead and consumption both charge work, including failed keyword attempts. Important-annotation lookahead follows the reference's first-character dispatch and leaves escape validation to its shared identifier reader.

Declarations are parsed before selector lookahead, with bounded rollback only for an ambiguous unspaced value. This prevents valid raw special-function contents from being rejected by selector delimiters. Custom properties share the raw result-value reader, preserve literal `//`, interpolation and glued name comments, and remain forbidden within nested properties. Leading-hyphen interpolation, property hacks, directive-specific indented whitespace, and case-insensitive `using` follow the reference. Selector headers and extension targets share the raw `almostAnyValue` reader: raw escapes, uninterpreted comments, URL tokens, bracket tracking, punctuation boundaries, and indented comma continuation. Raw brackets are iterative counts bounded by work, not recursive grammar calls. As in the reference, an unclosed raw selector bracket can reach EOF and retains selector evidence; no missing closer is invented. Unknown CSS rule values use the declaration-value grammar. The legacy lowercase `@-moz-document` rule validates its fixed `url`, `url-prefix`, `domain`, `regexp`, and interpolation grammar while retaining CSS evidence.

**Reference API clarification.** `sass-parser` 0.4.58 forces `parseSelectors: true`, while the compiler's normal parser uses `false`. It can reject valid keyframes or repeated combinators, and accept pseudo arguments such as `:foo(!)` that the normal parser rejects before evaluation. The previous facade expectation for that pseudo argument was incorrect and is corrected. Syntax closure uses the normal parser; the existing selector compilation oracle still checks the evaluation grammar. CSS function signatures, unknown CSS semantics, and evaluation-dependent grammars are deliberately outside Sass's parse-time validity contract, not unfinished Sass parsing. JavaScript diagnostic recovery remains separate in section 36.

**Errors and bounds.** Recognized syntax failures return a typed `SassError` with an original UTF-8 byte offset and no partial document. There is no Sass error recovery yet. `sass.max-nodes` defaults to 1,000,000 and bounds live syntax entries plus retained comments, including speculative lookahead; `sass.max-depth` defaults to 128 grammar calls and is capped at 128 even if configured higher; `sass.max-work` defaults to 16,777,216 reader visits, including lookahead and discarded work. Lookahead discards speculative nodes and comments, but never restores the work budget. `Limits::entries()` now lists 53 entries, including the JavaScript recovery limits of `D-085`. A limit error does not return the recovered or partially parsed tree.

**Validation commands.** The facade contract tests are in `crates/frameshift-engine/tests/sass.rs`, including grammar-directed SassScript cases, repeated parsing, exact node reachability, ordered and contained spans, explicit unchecked evidence, both surface syntaxes, invalid input, and resource boundaries. The `sass` fuzz target checks both modes, deterministic results and errors, valid coordinates, ordered and contained spans, complete arena reachability, and node bounds. Its permanent seeds are fictional.

The optional oracle requires external `sass-parser` 0.4.58 and Sass 1.105.2, checks their installed versions, and invokes only their parser. It never evaluates the inputs or loads imports:

```text
FRAMESHIFT_SASS_PARSER_MODULE=<external sass-parser package directory> cargo test -p frameshift-engine --offline --locked --test sass external_sass_oracle -- --ignored --nocapture
```

The URL classification oracle additionally compares raw string versus SassScript call structure, without evaluation or URL loading:

```text
FRAMESHIFT_SASS_PARSER_MODULE=<external sass-parser package directory> cargo test -p frameshift-engine --offline --locked --test sass external_url_classification_oracle -- --ignored --nocapture
```

Reference sources, npm packages, public conformance data, and temporary analysis helpers remain outside the repository. Broader corpus comparisons measure remaining grammar gaps; they are not evidence of complete conformance.

**Historical increment evidence.** The dated results below record each earlier revision; the current closure is documented under `D-090` below.

**External evidence (2026-10-21, initial parser revision `7d96c7d`).** The pinned parse-only oracle agrees on all 1,382 fictional facade cases, including 1,296 grammar-directed expression cases. A separate development driver read 15,750 source files or HRX source sections from sass-spec revision `58295762b6e8d9d2c22e6137bf9f0a70bb718c49`, outside the repository. Dart Sass's parser-export API accepted 15,188; Frameshift accepted 15,090. Of Frameshift's results, 14,310 carried unchecked-grammar evidence. All 173 documents that Frameshift accepted and the reference rejected carried such evidence; none of the 780 documents without unchecked entries disagreed with reference acceptance. Frameshift rejected 271 reference-accepted inputs, including one whose original bytes are not UTF-8. These comparisons locate remaining work, including differences in selector handling by the reference's parser-export API; they do not establish complete Sass conformance or migration safety.

**Local validation (2026-10-21, initial parser revision `7d96c7d`).** On Linux x86_64 with Rust 1.98.0, the offline locked workspace build and test suite pass: 487 tests, zero failures, and 16 deliberately ignored golden updaters or external runners. Formatting, all-target Clippy with warnings denied, the three E2 goldens, and all 3,000 generated E1 corpus contracts pass. Documentation links and typography pass. Native CI and repository policy checks remain the PR workflow's responsibility; no additional local native platform is claimed.

An instrumented initial 60-second `sass` smoke run completed 979,836 executions without findings. On parser revision `7d96c7d`, a fresh 120-second smoke run completed 1,249,755 executions, reached 7,531 coverage features, and retained 1,913 corpus inputs without findings. These are wall-clock smoke runs, not the CPU-hour budget of `D-044`.


**Mixin extension correction (2026-10-21, PR #30 review).** `@extend` is permitted in a mixin body and in control blocks inside that body, even before the mixin is included in a style rule. The context check accepts either an active style rule or mixin, while still rejecting root, function, and nested-property uses. A mixin without a parameter list also preserves its indented body boundary instead of consuming the newline as header whitespace. A regression test covers SCSS and indented Sass, direct and conditional mixin extensions, and the three forbidden contexts. The four positive cases also join the pinned parse-only oracle, for 1,386 fictional cases in total. The test fails on the original guard and passes with this correction.

The correction passes the Linux x86_64 offline locked workspace suite (488 tests, zero failures, 16 intentional ignored tests), formatting, all-target Clippy with warnings denied, and all 1,386 pinned parse-only oracle cases. The initial PR revision also passed all six native CI jobs, packaging, repository policies, and the determinism check in CI run `39527613606`; the corrected revision is checked by its own PR workflow.


**Indented selector correction (2026-10-21, PR #30 review).** Indented Sass interprets a colon immediately followed by an identifier or interpolation prefix as selector syntax, including an empty selector body. SCSS applies the same prefix disambiguation when a braced child block follows. The parser now shares identifier-start lookahead for ordinary, escaped, and hyphenated names, and preserves `a:hover` as a style rule rather than a nested property. Custom properties and property names followed by colon whitespace retain declaration semantics. Regression cases assert the arena structure for ordinary, interpolated, and hyphenated pseudo-classes, a media-query child, an empty body, and a hyphenated SCSS selector; the six additional oracle cases bring the total to 1,392. The indented media-query case is also a permanent fictional fuzz seed.

The selector correction passes the offline locked Linux x86_64 workspace suite (489 tests, zero failures, 16 intentional ignored tests), all 1,392 pinned oracle cases, the fuzz target's permanent seeds, and replay of the saved final-smoke corpus. Formatting, all-target Clippy, and documentation checks pass.

**Content-block correction (2026-10-21, PR #30 review).** Include bodies carry an explicit lexical content-block context. Properties, custom properties, and extensions are allowed there even when the include is at the stylesheet root. Nested callable declarations remain invalid; `@content` still requires an enclosing mixin. Nested includes and control blocks inherit content context, and leaving the block restores the enclosing context. Fourteen fictional cases cover both syntax modes, positive and negative contexts, and context restoration; all agree with the pinned parse-only oracle (1,406 cases total). The facade regression also checks that content properties produce declaration nodes and fails before the correction. The Sass facade suite passes 12 tests with one intentional external-oracle ignore.

**Comment-boundary correction (2026-10-21, PR #30 review).** Only comments consumed at statement boundaries, after indentation alone, use indented Sass continuation and optional loud-comment closure. Comments inside headers and expressions use inline rules, including when an expression's comment begins on a new line. Inline loud comments require `*/`, can cross indentation boundaries until that closer, and return `Unterminated` at EOF. Nine additional oracle cases cover missing and explicit closers, dedented text, expression continuations, and standalone comments. A regression checks exact comment spans and the following declaration and fails before this correction. Four additional permanent fuzz seeds cover include content and comment boundaries.

Both latest corrections pass the offline locked Linux x86_64 workspace suite (491 tests, zero failures, 16 intentional ignored tests), all 1,415 pinned parse-only oracle cases, permanent fuzz seeds, and saved-corpus replay. Formatting, all-target Clippy with warnings denied, documentation links, and typography checks pass. Full grammar conformance remains outstanding as described above.

**Legacy indented shorthand validation (2026-10-21, `D-080`).** Four facade tests add shared grammar and context cases, exact BOM/CRLF marker/name/body spans and sibling boundaries, dialect and selector-prefix disambiguation, and node/depth/work limits. The grammar and node-kind regressions fail before the change. The pinned parse-only oracle agrees on all 1,446 fictional cases, including 31 new shorthand and selector-boundary cases. Six permanent fictional fuzz seeds and three mutation tokens cover the new forms. A fresh instrumented 60-second `sass` smoke run completed 431,090 executions, reached 7,068 coverage features, and retained 1,623 inputs without findings; this is not the CPU-hour budget of `D-044`.

The Linux x86_64 offline locked workspace build and suite pass (495 tests, zero failures, 16 intentional ignores), as do formatting, all-target Clippy with warnings denied, and the three E2 goldens. Documentation links and typography pass. Native CI and repository policy checks run in the pull request workflow; full Sass conformance remains outstanding.

**Selectors (`D-081`).** `src/sass/selector.rs` replaces a style rule's raw selector text with structure after the existing header scan has fixed its extent, so statement boundaries and acceptance do not change. The first child of a `StyleRule` is a `SelectorList` of `ComplexSelector` nodes; a complex selector holds `CompoundSelector` and explicit `Combinator` nodes in source order, and adjacent compounds are separated by whitespace or comments. Simple selectors are `TypeSelector` and `UniversalSelector` with a `SassNamespace`, `ClassSelector`, `IdSelector`, `PlaceholderSelector`, `NestingSelector` (`&` with an optional suffix), `AttributeSelector` (namespace, optional `SassAttributeOperator`, value, and modifier), and `PseudoSelector` (`::` flag, name, raw `InterpolatedText` argument, nested `SelectorList`, or both for `nth-child` and `nth-last-child`). Keyframe blocks get a `KeyframeSelectorList` of `KeyframeSelector` nodes. Names are `Identifier` nodes, or `InterpolatedText` with interpolation children; spellings and escapes are preserved. List and complex spans end at their last selector or comma.

Grammar selection follows Dart Sass 1.105.2. An interpolation-free selector is parsed with the grammar evaluation applies to it: `SelectorParser`, including the `An+B` microsyntax, single-letter attribute modifiers, and repeated or dangling combinators, or `KeyframeSelectorParser` directly inside an at-rule whose plain name without vendor prefix is exactly `keyframes`. A selector with interpolation uses the parse-time interpolated grammar and carries `Selector` evidence; inside keyframes it stays raw. Mixin bodies, include content blocks, at-rules with interpolated names, style rules nested in keyframe blocks, and `@at-root` queries inside keyframes get an evaluation-dependent context: their selectors are parsed with the selector grammar and carry evidence. A selector that its grammar rejects stays the raw `InterpolatedText` with evidence and is not an error, because Dart Sass's own parse step accepts it and evaluation may never reach it; pseudo arguments with `!`, `;`, `{`, `}`, or `//`, and indented loud comments glued between non-whitespace characters, take this path conservatively. Each attempt runs within the raw extent, restores parser state on failure, and charges the shared node, depth, and work limits; a limit error still ends the parse. An inline `@at-root` selector is now a `StyleRule` child of the at-rule, `AtRootPrelude` evidence covers only queries, and `@extend` targets remain raw with `Selector` evidence. In indented syntax, comments between a trailing comma and the line end no longer end a continued header.

**Selector validation (2026-10-21, Windows x86_64).** Five facade tests cover 33 SCSS structure cases and an indented continuation case, keyframe selectors in both syntaxes, evaluation-dependent evidence and raw fallback, inline `@at-root` rules, and the depth and node limits. The opt-in compile oracle, which needs the same `FRAMESHIFT_SASS_PARSER_MODULE` and compiles only generated fictional cases without module or import rules, agrees on all 14,431 cases: a selector is structured without evidence exactly when pinned Sass 1.105.2 compiles it.

```text
FRAMESHIFT_SASS_PARSER_MODULE=<external sass-parser package directory> cargo test -p frameshift-engine --offline --locked --test sass external_sass_selector_oracle -- --ignored --nocapture
```

The parse-only oracle still agrees on all 1,446 cases. A development survey outside the repository compared this revision with `main` at `6030ad0`: acceptance is unchanged on the 148 SCSS files of the approved real-world sample (`D-043`) and on 15,740 SCSS and indented sources or HRX sections of the pinned sass-spec revision (15,108 accepted). In the sample, 581 of 622 style rule selectors are structured without evidence and 41 carry evidence, none raw. In sass-spec, 19,967 of 20,323 are structured without evidence (37 keyframe lists), 307 carry evidence, and 49 stay raw; the raw selectors outside error cases are interpolated keyframes, interpolated at-rule names, and escaped `An+B` keywords, which Sass 1.105.2 rejects although that newer sass-spec revision expects them. Seventeen sass-spec documents without any unchecked entry are rejected by `sass-parser`; Sass 1.105.2 compiles all of them (repeated combinators, trailing and extra commas, indented pseudo whitespace).

The offline locked workspace build and suite pass (496 tests, zero failures, 17 intentional ignores), as do formatting, all-target Clippy with warnings denied, the three E2 goldens, the 3,000 generated corpus contracts, and the repository policy checker. The `sass` fuzz target now also checks selector kinds, unchecked spans, and evidence for every raw selector; eight fictional seeds and 22 tokens cover selectors. An instrumented 120-second smoke run completed 2,012,750 executions, reached 11,732 coverage features, and retained 3,017 inputs without findings; this is not the CPU-hour budget of `D-044`. Full Sass conformance remains outstanding for the other unchecked grammars above.

The pinned parse-only oracle agrees on all 12,213 fictional cases. The selector compile oracle also passes all 14,431 cases. Two independent regression checks, rejection of an invalid default namespace and acceptance of a literal interpolation marker in a forwarding URL, fail against `origin/main` at `f25fbc3` and pass with this implementation.

The offline locked workspace build and test suite pass (504 tests, zero failures, 17 intentional ignores), as do formatting, all-target Clippy with warnings denied, the three E2 goldens, the 3,000 generated E1 corpus contracts, and documentation links and typography. **Import review clarification (2026-10-21, PR #34).** The pinned Dart Sass parser and compiler both preserve `@import "theme-#{$name}.css";` as literal CSS with the interpolation marker unchanged, and reject SCSS `@import \75rl(theme);` with "Expected string." Internal escapes such as `u\72l(theme)` remain valid. In indented Sass, the leading-escape form is instead a literal unquoted Sass path. The import dispatch deliberately reproduces this asymmetry; it does not extend URL recognition beyond the pinned implementation. One targeted regression and 50 additional oracle cases preserve these boundaries. The parse-only oracle now agrees on 13,931 cases and the classification oracle on 1,922 cases; all 14,431 selector cases still pass.

The review clarification passes the offline locked workspace suite (508 tests, zero failures, 18 intentional ignores), formatting, all-target Clippy with warnings denied, and documentation checks. Product behavior is unchanged.

## 36. JavaScript diagnostic recovery (`D-034`, `D-085`, `D-088`, `D-089`)

`frameshift_engine::syntax::parse_js_program_with_recovery(source, goal, dialect, ParseLimits, RecoveryLimits)` is implemented in `crates/frameshift-syntax/src/parser/recovery.rs`. The existing `parse_js_program` and `parse_js_expression` contracts are unchanged: one error and no tree. The new API returns `JsProgramParse`, whose `tree()` and `into_tree()` expose a complete tree only if the original source has no error. An erroneous result retains original-coordinate diagnostics and withholds every syntax node. It never inserts tokens, edits source text, or makes a recovered region eligible for transformation.

**Boundaries.** The first diagnostic is the strict parser's exact error. The parser records the start of each outer program item and the root directive prologue's strictness. After an error, an iterative synchronizer scans only that failed item, consuming complete quoted strings and comments and balancing parentheses, brackets, and braces. It resumes after an explicit semicolon at delimiter depth zero, at or after the diagnostic, or finishes at balanced EOF. Newlines are not synchronization points. It stops instead of guessing at templates, non-comment slashes, `<`, escapes outside strings, top-level colons (labels, type annotations, or ternaries), control-flow or declaration keywords with nested statement grammar, an initial block or directive-like construct, unmatched delimiters, unterminated strings/comments, lexical errors, and deferred errors pointing before the current item. In Module goal, an initial `import` followed by a quote, binding identifier, `{`, or `*`, and an initial `export` followed by `const`, `let`, `var`, `{`, or `*` pass the keyword filter (`D-088`). Whitespace and comments before that next token are consumed under the same shared byte budget, and identifiers must end at a real identifier boundary. The remainder still uses the ordinary conservative synchronizer, including delimiter balance and lexical stops. An initial `export default` also passes the prefix filter (`D-089`), charging each keyword byte and its trivia to the same budget and requiring a complete identifier boundary. Its expression still uses the ordinary conservative scan: function/class declaration prefixes, dynamic imports, `import.meta`, TypeScript declaration prefixes, import attributes introduced by `with`, and every existing lexical or statement ambiguity stop recovery. An incomplete prefix at EOF stops too. The conservative keyword filter can stop at a keyword used as a property or binding name. Nested statement lists are not independently recovered. JavaScript, JSX, TypeScript, TSX, declaration-file, Script, and Module choices remain explicit; a valid file in any mode produces the same tree as the strict API.

**Context and completeness.** After a synchronization boundary, a fresh parser consumes the remaining original source at its original offset, preserving root strictness, source goal, and dialect. A string after a failed item cannot start a new directive prologue. Failed arenas, declaration scopes, and export bookkeeping are discarded, not repaired. Cross-batch duplicate declarations and export errors are not checked, and unresolved local exports in a resumed batch are not reported, since their declarations may have been in an earlier batch. Diagnostics therefore explain discovered errors without claiming completeness. A deferred error that would move diagnostic order backwards stops recovery. Every resumed attempt advances the source offset; already successful prefixes are not reparsed.

**Bounds and errors.** `RecoveryLimits::max_diagnostics` defaults to 256 (`parse.recovery.max-diagnostics`), and `max_work` to 1,048,576 visited synchronization bytes (`parse.recovery.max-work`). The inclusive byte budget is charged before advancing at UTF-8 boundaries, including inside strings and comments. Parser node, token, and comment allowances are shared across all attempts; TypeScript speculative token work is retained in that accounting. Each attempt retains the original nesting bound, which also bounds the synchronizer's iterative delimiter stack. The engine's canonical limit set now has 53 entries. Zero diagnostics returns a typed resource error before parsing; allocation failure to retain diagnostics returns a typed allocation error. Other stops retain collected diagnostics and a `RecoveryStop { offset, kind }`: `UnsafeBoundary`, `DiagnosticLimit`, `WorkLimit`, or `ParseFailure`. A stop of `None` means that parsing reached EOF, not that an erroneous file became valid.

**Validation.** Fourteen public-facade tests in `crates/frameshift-engine/tests/recovery.rs` cover strict-tree equality in every dialect and goal, multiple original Unicode/CRLF offsets, whole-string/comment/group synchronization, refusal at unsafe lexical and statement boundaries, root strictness and directive handling, cross-batch export limitations, cumulative parse and recovery limits, and deterministic diagnostic truncation on 1,000 invalid declarations. Module-prefix cases check original first errors, continued diagnostics in every dialect, source-goal isolation, comments and strings containing semicolons, unsafe prefixes and lexical contexts, repeatability, and every recovery-work cutoff through Unicode text. The `program` fuzz target now checks recovery in every dialect and goal: identical first errors, repeated diagnostics and stops, ordered valid coordinates, bounded diagnostic storage, exact valid-program arenas, and no tree for erroneous source. Permanent fictional seeds cover multiple failures, default-export expressions, and unsafe regular-expression and HTML-comment boundaries. Default-export tests include all dialects, expression strings and balanced objects, incomplete prefixes and keyword lookalikes, every prefix-trivia byte cutoff, module strictness, and preservation of the first strict-parser error (`D-089`).

**Local validation (2026-10-22, Linux x86_64).** The offline locked workspace suite passes 533 tests with zero failures and 20 intentional ignores. The workspace build, formatting, all-target Clippy with warnings denied, all three E2 goldens, and the 3,000 generated E1 corpus contracts pass. All five pinned external Sass oracles pass: 28,420 parse-only cases, 14,431 selector compilations, 3,301 condition compilations, 1,922 import classifications, and 213 extension/query compilations. Documentation links and typography pass. A 60-second instrumented `program` smoke run, including diagnostic recovery, completed 104,934 executions, reached 25,360 features, and retained 3,590 inputs without findings. The final 60-second instrumented `sass` smoke run, with the media evidence checker recognizing modern `if()` and a dedicated media seed, completed 604,263 executions, reached 18,067 features, and retained 4,313 inputs without findings. Smoke runs are wall-clock checks, not the CPU-hour budget of `D-044`. Native platform checks and repository policy validation run in the PR workflow.

**D-088 local validation (2026-10-22, Linux x86_64, implementation commit `21cee62`).** The offline locked workspace build and suite pass: 548 tests, zero failures, and 22 intentional golden-update or external-runner ignores. ## 37. M3 closure and the M4 module graph (`D-091`)

**M3 closure.** The owner selected closing M3 and moving directly to module resolution and the import graph on 2026-10-23. The capability review finds every M3 deliverable implemented:

| Capability | Existing evidence and contract |
| --- | --- |
| Complete JavaScript, JSX, TypeScript, TSX, and declaration-file parsing | Sections 4 and 6; accepted E1 under `D-047`, generated and external oracle checks, exact spans and comment lists |
| CSS tokenization and generic structural parsing | Sections 33 and 34; pinned CSS Syntax draft, external suite and differential evidence, explicit recovery diagnostics |
| SCSS and indented Sass syntax | Section 35 and `D-090`; 15,740 pinned external corpus sources/sections and 46,842 fictional normal-parser cases, no unexplained acceptance differences |
| Positions, comments, and resource safety | SourceText positions and per-file comment lists; explicit central limits and whole-file error contracts |
| Safe diagnostic recovery | Section 36 and `D-085`; conservative JavaScript boundaries and no erroneous tree, plus CSS bounded recovery |
| Reproducible fuzzing | Section 7; coverage-guided targets, retained fictional seeds, deterministic checks and recorded platform smoke runs |

The prior external conformance results are retained evidence, not new runs in this change. The current workspace contracts, E1 generated checks, and E2 goldens are rerun for this increment. M3 completion takes effect with green integration of this change. Nested-list diagnostic recovery is optional. CSS property semantics, evaluated Sass constructs, bindings, and runtime behavior are later analysis obligations, not missing syntax productions. M4 is in progress and `analyze` remains unavailable.

**API and boundaries.** `frameshift_engine::modules` exposes `Resolver`, `ResolutionConfig`, `build_module_graph`, `ModuleGraph`, and explicit result/error types. `Resolver::new` accepts a complete set of canonical admitted paths, an optional selected-package directory, parsed configuration, and `ModuleLimits`; it performs no I/O. `build_module_graph(snapshot, package_root, GraphLimits)` uses only `Snapshot::read`, rejects incomplete inventories, and never executes source, opens installed dependencies, follows links, or reads above the run root. Declaration recognition includes standard `.d.ts`/`.d.mts`/`.d.cts` and TypeScript's arbitrary-extension `.d.<extension>.ts` forms; every such file uses the ambient dialect. Nodes include admitted `.js`, `.jsx`, `.mjs`, `.cjs`, `.ts`, `.tsx`, `.mts`, `.cts`, and declaration files inside the selected package, in path-byte order. Sibling workspace code stays outside the local graph. `.cjs` uses Script goal; TypeScript surfaces, including `.cts` and `.d.cts`, use Module goal with the implementation or ambient dialect.

**Lookup profile.** This initial explicit source-lookup profile follows TypeScript 6.0.3 bundler lookup for local code: extension substitution, implicit implementation/declaration extensions, and directory indexes. `.js` uses `.ts`, `.tsx`, `.d.ts`, `.js`, then `.jsx`; `.jsx` and `.tsx` put `.tsx` before `.ts`, and `.mjs`/`.cjs` use their corresponding typed/declaration families. A failed substitution can try appended implicit extensions. Directory requests ending in `/`, `/.`, or `/..`, and literal `.` or `..`, bypass file substitution and retain directory intent through path aliases and base-URL lookup. JSON requires an explicit `.json` suffix and can resolve a `.d.json.ts` declaration first. For arbitrary extensions, `.d.<extension>.ts` declarations and implicit code candidates precede the exact asset. Exact stylesheet and other asset references remain a fallback when there is no typed target, classified only by extension. This establishes the referenced snapshot file, not valid content, successful type checking, or the behavior of custom webpack/Turbopack configurations. A directory with an admitted `package.json` uses the entry-point semantics of section 38.

**Configuration.** The builder selects `tsconfig.json`, then `jsconfig.json`, in the selected package root, without falling back past an excluded configuration. JSONC parsing rejects duplicate keys and malformed setting shapes. `compilerOptions.baseUrl` and `paths` support exact aliases, a single wildcard, exact-match then longest-prefix priority, and ordered substitution fallback before base-URL lookup and external classification. An empty `baseUrl` or `./` denotes the originating configuration directory, including through inheritance. One trailing slash in `baseUrl` is accepted; absolute paths and repeated empty components remain unsupported. Relative `extends` strings and arrays load through the snapshot, with optional `.json` only when the exact candidate is absent rather than excluded and the requested path does not already end in `.json`, ordered inheritance, whole-map path overrides, and correct originating directories. Configuration may inherit from elsewhere inside the run root, but resolved source cannot leave the selected package. Cycles, missing/unreadable configurations, unsupported package-based inheritance, and unsupported resolver options remain structured `ConfigIssue` records and suppress resolution. Absent module resolution selects the bundler profile; `node` and `node10` select the separate profile of section 42. Other modes and `rootDirs`, `moduleSuffixes`, `customConditions`, `noResolve`, and package-resolution overrides return unsupported evidence. Section 38 adds `module`, `target`, `allowJs`, `checkJs`, `outDir`, and `declarationDir`, each inherited separately.

**Edges and errors.** Every strict successful parse supplies exact original-coordinate spans and decoded literal specifiers for static imports, source-bearing named/star re-exports, dynamic imports, TypeScript import types, and import-equals references. Explicit whole-declaration type-only syntax and declaration-file dependencies set `type_only`. Inline type specifiers do not imply runtime erasure. Computed/unrepresentable specifiers stay unresolved. Calls spelling `require`, and calls through escaped identifiers that might spell it, retain `RequireCandidate` evidence without claiming a binding. Invalid UTF-8, source-text limits, unreadable source, and parser errors are per-module states; syntax errors supply no partial edges. Snapshot corruption, cumulative limits, or an invalid selected root return no graph. Other local references, external package names, `node:` references, and unresolved reasons remain separate `Resolution` variants. Bare-package classification does not prove that a dependency is declared or installed. Package `imports` (`#...`), self-references, directory package entry points, and each edge's resolution mode are specified in section 38. Binding analysis and render-context propagation are implemented in sections 39 and 40; custom bundler rules remain M4 work.

`ModuleGraph::closure` traverses iteratively, handles cycles, includes the starting node, and returns nodes in path order. It optionally includes explicitly type-only edges. It does not infer type erasure, validate imports/exports against bindings, prove runtime completeness, or discard unresolved evidence; callers must inspect node states, edge resolutions, and declaration targets. `collect_dependencies(tree, path, resolver, max_edges)` extracts the same facts from an existing strict syntax tree without reparsing; later semantic stages can share a syntax cache through this API. The convenience snapshot builder retains graph facts and drops per-file syntax arenas after extraction.

**Limits.** Four entries bring `Limits::entries` to 53. `Limits::modules()` bundles them with the existing text, path, JSONC, and JavaScript parser limits.

| Entry | Inclusive default |
| --- | --- |
| `modules.max-files` | 100,000 admitted paths |
| `modules.max-edges` | 1,000,000 occurrences across the graph |
| `modules.max-config-depth` | 32 active inheritance levels, implementation ceiling 64 |
| `modules.max-work` | 268,435,456 charged units |

Work charges cover indexed path bytes, configuration/source/manifest bytes before parsing, configuration visits, graph-node visits, alias matching/substitution bytes, package-scope probes, package-map keys and conditions, and candidate-probe bytes. Parser-internal work retains its existing per-file limits. Budget exhaustion never returns a partial graph. Stable IDs are source paths plus original spans, not traversal counters.

**Validation.** Public-facade contracts are in `crates/frameshift-engine/tests/module_graph.rs`, including permanent regressions for typed-extension priority and implicit fallback. The ignored oracle compares 4,655 fictional local resolution requests across seven configurations with the pinned TypeScript 6.0.3 resolver, using an in-memory host. It executes only the external reference package and generated requests, never source-project code:

```text
FRAMESHIFT_TYPESCRIPT_MODULE=<external typescript package directory> cargo test -p frameshift-engine --offline --locked --test module_graph external_typescript_bundler_oracle -- --ignored --nocapture
```

The `modules` fuzz target checks arbitrary UTF-8 references and JSONC settings for repeatability, path-order independence, admitted targets, package containment, configuration refusal, and work-budget failures. It uses only fictional in-memory file lists. Native tests, boundary policy, packaging, and cross-platform checks run in the pull request workflow. No external runtime dependency or new CLI/document schema is added.


**Local validation (2026-10-23, Linux x86_64).** The offline locked workspace build and debug suite pass, as do formatting, warnings-denied workspace/all-target Clippy, the three E2 goldens, all 3,000 generated E1 corpus contracts, and documentation links and typography. The final module suite passes 24 ordinary contract tests, with the external oracle intentionally ignored in ordinary runs. The separately selected pinned TypeScript 6.0.3 oracle passes all 4,655 cases. The source-order budget regression distinguishes an immediate edge limit at an early import from exhaustion in a later large expression; arbitrary-extension declaration cases are checked against the reference compiler's declaration-file classification.

An instrumented 60-second `modules` smoke run on code commit `7d85007` completes 558,338 executions, reaches 18,618 coverage features, and retains 4,212 inputs without findings. After the source-order and declaration-classification corrections, an instrumented rebuild replays all 4,197 saved corpus mutations successfully; the remaining 15 inputs are built-in seeds, also exercised by the ordinary fuzzer test suite. This is wall-clock smoke/replay evidence, not the CPU-hour budget of `D-044` or a throughput benchmark. The final native six-platform suites, packaging, and policy checks run in PR #43; no additional local platform or local PowerShell-policy execution is claimed.

**PR #43 review corrections (2026-10-23).** Three regression tests first reproduced all three review findings, then passed after correction. Arbitrary-extension `.d.<extension>.ts` declarations now precede implicit code candidates and exact assets, including declaration-only imports. An excluded exact `extends` candidate now reports a configuration issue instead of selecting its `.json` sibling. Empty `baseUrl` values denote the originating configuration directory, including inherited settings. The expanded pinned resolver oracle agrees on 3,280 cases across five configurations; all 21 ordinary module tests, the 584-test workspace suite (25 intentional ignores), formatting, and warnings-denied workspace/all-target Clippy pass. The existing 4,197 saved fuzz mutations replay successfully. One trailing configuration slash is now accepted without allowing absolute or repeated-empty paths. Imports ending in a slash, dot, or parent component preserve directory intent through relative lookup, path aliases, and base-URL lookup.
