## 38. Package scopes, `imports`, self-references, and directory entry points (`D-092`)

Implemented as the first block of `D-092` in `crates/frameshift-modules/src/package.rs`, with the resolver and graph changes in `resolve.rs`, `config.rs`, and `graph.rs`. This section describes TypeScript 6.0.3 `bundler` lookup for local code; the separate Node10 profile is described in section 42. The reference functions named below are those of that release's `typescript.js`.

**Manifests.** `PackageManifest::parse` reads a `package.json` as strict JSON (`D-068`) and retains only `name`, `typings`, `types`, `main`, the presence of an object or array `typesVersions`, `exports`, and `imports`. Invalid JSON, duplicate keys, or a non-object top level return `None`. Path fields that are empty strings or not strings are absent, as `readPackageJsonPathField` treats them. Map objects keep JavaScript own-key order: canonical array-index keys first in numeric order, then insertion order. `Resolver::add_manifest` supplies one admitted manifest, or `None` for an invalid or unreadable one; the snapshot graph builder reads, through `Snapshot::read`, every manifest inside the selected package and every manifest above it inside the run root. A manifest that is admitted but not supplied is never guessed.

**Resolution mode.** `Resolver::mode(importer, kind)` assigns `ResolutionMode::Import` or `Require` as `getModeForUsageLocation` does in bundler resolution: import-equals and `require` candidates use `Require`; `.cjs`, `.cts`, and `.d.cts` files use `Require` and `.mjs`, `.mts`, and `.d.mts` files `Import` for every other reference; otherwise the emitted format decides, from `module` or, without it, from `target` (an ES5 target emits CommonJS, every other target or none an ECMAScript module). A dynamic import uses `Import` under `module: preserve`. A whole-declaration `import type` or `export type` with exactly one string-keyed attribute `"resolution-mode"` whose value is `import` or `require`, and an import type with `{ with: { "resolution-mode": ... } }`, override the mode; any other attribute shape keeps the default, as `getResolutionModeOverride` does. Each `Edge` records its `mode`. `resolve` uses the static-import mode, and `resolve_in_mode` takes an explicit one. In the bundler profile, `module` values `none`, `amd`, `umd`, and `system` (classic resolution) and `node16`, `node18`, `node20`, and `nodenext` (Node.js resolution and package-scope formats) are unsupported, after inheritance has combined the files; unknown `module` or `target` values and non-boolean `allowJs` or `checkJs` are invalid.

**Lookup order.** A non-relative request tries `paths`, then `baseUrl`, then package `imports` for a `#` specifier, then a self-reference, then bare classification, as `nodeModuleNameResolverWorker` orders them. Package scopes are the nearest `package.json` at or above the importer's directory inside the run root (`getPackageScopeForPath`). Conditions are `import` or `require` by mode, plus `types` and `default`; `node`, `browser`, and other conditions never match.

**Package `imports`.** `#` alone continues the lookup, and `#/` is accepted, as the bundler feature set allows. An exact key matches a request without `*` or a trailing `/`; otherwise keys with one `*` or a trailing `/` are tried in `comparePatternKeys` order (longer bases by UTF-16 length, patterns before folder keys, then longer keys), and the first matching key decides, including trailer patterns such as `#a/*.js` and the reference's overlap capture for names shorter than a trailer key. Targets follow `loadModuleFromTargetExportOrImport`: strings, conditions in own-key order, arrays whose first decisive element wins, and `null`, which maps the request to `MappedToNull`. A `./` target must not contain `.`, `..`, or `node_modules` components after its first, nor may the captured subpath; `*` is replaced with JavaScript `replace` semantics, including `$$`, `$&`, and `$'`. The final path is probed exactly for explicit TypeScript and declaration extensions and otherwise only by extension substitution, so an extensionless target never resolves. A bare string target restarts the whole lookup from the scope directory with the same mode. A request that no key maps, or whose targets all fail, is `NotFound`, because package names never start with `#` and installed contents are never read.

**Self-references.** A bare request whose leading components equal the scope's string `name` resolves through `exports` when the scope has a truthy `exports` field (`loadModuleFromSelfNameReference`), with subpath `.` or `./<rest>`. String, array, and condition-only `exports` serve `.`; subpath maps apply only when every key starts with `.`. Without `allowJs` (or `checkJs`), a first pass accepts only TypeScript and declaration targets and a second pass JavaScript and JSON, so a `types` condition can win over an earlier JavaScript-only `import` target. A failed self-reference continues to bare classification; a `null` entry ends it.

**Directory entry points.** For a local directory with an admitted manifest, `typings`, then `types`, then `main` select one path (`loadNodeModuleFromDirectoryWorker`). It is probed as a package field, then as a file with substitution and implicit extensions, then as a directory index; when it yields nothing, the directory's own index follows. A missing `typings` or `types` target does not fall back to `main`. Field paths are normalized with separators unified and `.` and `..` resolved; a rooted path or one that leaves the run root is `OutsideRoot`.

**Fail-closed boundaries.** `typesVersions` returns `DirectoryPackage`; a manifest that is invalid, unreadable, excluded, or not supplied returns `PackageManifest`, also for every bare request under that scope, because a self-reference cannot be excluded. A scope above the run root is not visible: `#` requests are then `PackageManifest` and self-references do not match. Versioned `types@` conditions, a `` $` `` replacement in a `./` target, and bare targets nested more than 16 times return `PackageImports`; the reference has no nesting bound and overflows its stack on a self-mapping. `outDir` or `declarationDir` returns `Configuration` for package-map targets, because the reference maps output paths back to inputs through project-root inference. Package-map targets that leave the selected package are `OutsideRoot`. As for relative requests, a stylesheet or other asset is a Frameshift fallback after its `.d.<extension>.ts` declaration; the reference resolves only the declaration.

**Validation.** `crates/frameshift-engine/tests/package_scopes.rs` holds six facade contracts: import-map keys, conditions, arrays, nulls, bare and recursive targets, and refusals; self-references in both `allowJs` modes; directory entry-point precedence and refusals; mode assignment by extension, edge kind, and emitted format; output-directory and invalid-scope refusals; and a snapshot graph with nested manifests, attribute overrides, and per-edge modes. Two unit tests check key priority, overlap capture, and array-index ordering. The ignored oracle compares 10,428 generated fictional requests (four importer extensions, three reference kinds, six configurations) with the pinned resolver, which computes each mode itself from a one-line source file; 4,987 resolve to local files and 5,846 use `require` mode, which the test asserts to rule out a vacuous comparison. Requests the oracle resolves to nothing and Frameshift resolves to an asset are the documented fallback. Deliberately reversing the key order or dropping the `types` condition produced 66 and 272 differences:

```text
FRAMESHIFT_TYPESCRIPT_MODULE=<external typescript package directory> cargo test -p frameshift-engine --offline --locked --test package_scopes external_typescript_package_scope_oracle -- --ignored --nocapture
```

The `modules` fuzz target also reads its first line as the manifest of the root and of a nested package directory, and its seeds include import maps, conditions, self-references, and replacement patterns. An instrumented 60-second smoke run on macOS arm64 completes 1,072,451 executions, reaches 22,858 coverage features, and retains 5,404 inputs without findings; this is smoke evidence, not the CPU-hour budget of `D-044`. The earlier module oracle of section 37 still agrees on all 4,655 cases. On macOS, the existing discovery test `special_files_are_skipped` fails locally only because the temporary socket path of this worktree exceeds the platform's `SUN_LEN`; it is unrelated to this change and passes in native CI.


## 39. Scopes and bindings (`D-092`)

Implemented as the second block of `D-092` in `crates/frameshift-bindings`, exposed as `frameshift_engine::bindings`. `analyze(tree)` takes one strict `SyntaxTree` and returns `Bindings`: scopes, bindings, references, and module exports. It reads nothing but the tree, never resolves modules or evaluates code, and has no framework knowledge. `SyntaxTree::identifier_value` (syntax crate) supplies the decoded StringValue of identifier, binding, label, private, and JSX names.

**Model.** A `Scope` has a `ScopeKind` (script, module, function with an `arrow` flag, function-expression name, class, static block, block, catch, `with`, namespace, ambient module, `declare global`, enum, and type scopes for type parameters, signatures, mapped types, and conditional `infer`), its node, parent, strictness, and a `direct_eval` flag. A `Binding` is one name in one scope with every `Declaration` it merges, in source order; each declaration records its `DeclarationKind`, name node, declaring construct, and ambient state. Meanings follow TypeScript's value, type, and namespace spaces: variables, functions, and parameters are values; classes are values and types; enums all three; interfaces, aliases, and type parameters types; a namespace is a namespace, and also a value when its body instantiates it (anything but types, interfaces, type-only syntax, and uninstantiated namespaces); imports and `import =` aliases match every meaning. One binding per scope and name merges compatible declarations, such as overloads, interface and class merges, and a value with a type of the same name. Import bindings carry an `Import` with the specifier literal, the decoded specifier, the `ImportedName` (default, named, namespace, or `require`), and the type-only flag. Namespace exports, enum members, and merged interface or class type parameters live in the container binding's member table, so merged declarations share them.

**Resolution.** One iterative pass with an explicit stack declares every name in its scope, as `var`, function, and lexical rules require, and records each reference with its scope and meaning; references resolve only after all declarations exist, so hoisting needs no second pass. A reference walks its scope chain and takes the first binding whose meaning overlaps. Identifiers in expressions are value reads; assignment, update, `for-in`/`for-of`, and destructuring targets are writes, and compound assignments and updates also reads. Type references, `implements` and interface `extends` names, qualified-name prefixes (namespace meaning), `typeof` queries (value meaning), type-predicate parameters, and `import =` entities are references in their spaces; property, member, and export-from names are not. JSX tag names are value references unless they are intrinsic: a single name that starts with a lowercase ASCII letter or contains a hyphen, as TypeScript and the React transform decide; member tags reference their leftmost name, also through type arguments. Export specifiers without `from` reference all meanings. The implicit `arguments` binding of the nearest non-arrow function is created on first use.

**ECMAScript rules.** In sloppy code, a function declaration in a block, `switch` case, or Annex B `if` clause is bound in the block and, unless a lexical declaration between the block and the variable scope, or a parameter of the same name, would make `var` an early error, also as an `AnnexBFunction` declaration in the variable scope (B.3.2, B.3.3); strict code keeps it block-scoped. A named function expression binds its name in its own `FunctionName` scope; a named class expression in its class scope. A value reference that passes a `with` body, or the variable scope of a sloppy direct `eval` (a non-optional call of an unbound `eval`, also parenthesized), resolves to `Target::Dynamic` instead of a guessed binding. A name that no scope binds is `Target::Global`. `declare global` declarations are visible throughout the file.

**TypeScript rules.** Parameter lists and return types see only the function's parameters (value meaning) and type parameters (type meaning), not declarations of its body. Static class members cannot reference the class's type parameters. Merged interfaces and classes share type parameters by name, but each declaration sees only the ones it declares itself. Namespace bodies see their merged exports, and enum bodies their members. Enum members are value and type bindings of their enum. Type parameters of `infer` belong to their conditional type's true branch, and mapped keys to their mapped type. Parameters and the body share one function scope; a body `var` with a parameter's name merges into the parameter's binding, but a parameter default cannot see another body declaration, as ECMAScript's separate variable environment for parameter expressions requires. The reference checker binds a body `var` there; generated programs have no parameter defaults, so the oracle does not exercise this documented difference.

**Exports.** `exports()` lists, in source order, local declarations (`Exported` names and `default`), specifier exports with their resolved local target, anonymous default and `export =` expressions, re-exports of names or namespaces, and `export *`, each with its node and type-only flag. Exported declarations in namespaces become container members instead.

**Limits.** The analysis is linear in the tree's nodes, with name lookups along a scope chain whose depth the parser's nesting bound limits, so the tree's `ParseLimits` bound it and no separate entry joins the central limit set. Storage reservation is fallible, and indices beyond 32 bits return `TooLarge`. Deep expression nesting does not recurse.

**Validation.** `crates/frameshift-engine/tests/bindings.rs` holds seven facade contracts: lexical shadowing and hoisting, patterns with writes and `arguments`, Annex B with dynamic `with` and `eval` capture, imports, exports, and JSX tags, TypeScript meanings, merging, and type scopes, binding kinds and the declaration index, and a 50,000-deep parenthesized expression. The ignored oracle in `tools/frameshift-corpus/tests/bindings_oracle.rs` compares every reference with the pinned TypeScript 6.0.3 checker (`getSymbolAtLocation`, with shorthand properties and export specifiers through their local targets, `noLib`, sloppy scripts), and reports identifiers that the checker binds to a local declaration but the analysis misses:

```text
FRAMESHIFT_TYPESCRIPT_MODULE=<external typescript package directory> cargo test -p frameshift-corpus --offline --locked --test bindings_oracle -- --ignored --nocapture
```

`FRAMESHIFT_BINDING_ORACLE_FILES` sets the files per generated corpus (default 200). With 1,000, the comparison covers 5,005 fictional files: the generated JavaScript, JSX component, and TypeScript corpora, five hand-written edge-case files, and 2,000 generated programs that redeclare a four-name pool in nested scopes. All 515,801 references agree (344,333 bound), apart from 436 at names that the checker itself reports as redeclared (diagnostics 2300, 2451, and related), where it keeps a conflicting declaration outside the symbol; the TypeScript dialects defer redeclaration errors to the checker, as the reference parser does. Three further reference behaviors are excluded by kind, not by result: JavaScript expando assignments such as `Promise.items = 1`, which the checker infers as declarations, export specifiers, which are aliases, and a stack overflow of the checker on generated recursive types, after which a file is skipped. Moving `let` to the variable scope, or dropping the catch scope, produced 628 and 92 differences on 300 files per corpus.

The `bindings` fuzz target (section 7) checks every accepted tree. An instrumented 60-second smoke run on macOS arm64 completes 753,124 executions, reaches 26,670 coverage features, and retains 6,179 inputs without findings; this is smoke evidence, not the CPU-hour budget of `D-044`. Binding analysis adds no dependency and no CLI or document schema; it is a library fact set for the later component graph, render contexts, and the static evaluator.

## 40. App Router route tree, convention exports, and render contexts (`D-092`)

Implemented as the third block of `D-092` in `crates/frameshift-nextjs` (`src/routes.rs`, `src/contexts.rs`, `src/app.rs`), with directive prologues and a tree visitor in `frameshift-modules`. The rules follow the Next.js 16.4.2 distribution: `collectAppFiles` and its valid-file matcher, `normalizeAppPath`, `getSegmentParam`, the segment file types of the app loader, and `isMetadataRouteFile`. Nothing is evaluated, and no file is read except through the snapshot.

**Module graph additions.** Every parsed `ModuleNode` now records its module prologue and every function-body prologue as `Directive` values (decoded value, an `escaped` flag for spellings with a backslash, the literal span, and the enclosing function body). `build_module_graph_with(snapshot, root, limits, visit)` builds the same graph and lets `visit` inspect each strict tree once before its arena is dropped; an error from `visit` returns no graph. `build_module_graph` is that function with a visitor that does nothing.

**Route tree.** `app_routes(inventory, package, app, extensions)` reads the admitted files below a traversed app directory, skipping every entry whose name starts with `_` at any depth, as `collectAppFiles` does. `convention` classifies a file name: `page`, `route`, `layout`, `template`, `loading`, `error`, `not-found`, `forbidden`, `unauthorized`, and `default` with one of the page extensions in any directory; `global-error` and `global-not-found` only in the app root; and metadata files: `favicon.ico`, `robots` (`.txt` or code), and `manifest` (`.json`, `.webmanifest`, or code) only in the app root, `sitemap` (`.xml` or code) anywhere, and `icon`, `apple-icon`, `opengraph-image`, and `twitter-image` with an optional single-digit variant and their image extensions or code anywhere. `segment` classifies a folder as a group `(name)` first, then a parallel slot `@name`, then strips an interception marker (`(..)(..)`, `(.)`, `(..)`, `(...)`, in that order) and recognizes `[[...name]]`, `[...name]`, and `[name]`. `pattern` drops groups and slots, as `normalizeAppPath` does, so an interception marker stays in the pattern. Package-level `instrumentation`, `middleware`, and `proxy` files beside the app directory, or beside `src/app` in `src`, are convention files too.

Each page, route handler, and metadata file is an `AppRoute` with its pattern and segments; a metadata pattern ends with the file name without a code extension. A page's chain lists, from the app root down to its own directory, the layout, template, error, loading, not-found, forbidden, and unauthorized files of every enclosing directory, including groups and slots; `layouts()` selects the layouts and `in_slot()` reports a slot other than `@children`. `RouteIssue` records a directory with both `page` and `route`, pages or handlers outside slots that share a pattern, a page without any layout, several files of one convention that differ only in extension, and every entry below the app directory that was not read as a file or directory (ignored, oversized, a link, or untraversed), because Next.js may read files there.

**Convention exports.** `analyze_app(snapshot, package, app, extensions, limits)` builds the route tree and the package's module graph with one parse per file, runs binding analysis (section 39) on every convention file, and records its sorted export names, whether `export *` adds names only its source knows, and whether the default export is an expression. Names such as `generateStaticParams`, `metadata`, `dynamic`, or `GET` identify Next.js facets; their values remain for the static evaluator.

**Render contexts.** `render_contexts(graph, routes)` starts every convention file in the server context, or in the client context when its own prologue holds `"use client"`, and follows runtime edges only: explicitly type-only edges are erased. A `"use client"` module reached from the server context is a `Boundary` and runs in the client context with everything it imports; a `"use server"` module runs in the server context from either side. Directives with escapes are not directives, as for `"use strict"`, and are reported. A `ModuleContext` per reached module records the server and client flags and its own directives, so a module imported from both sides runs in both. `ContextIssue` records reached modules without a strict parse, unresolved runtime references of reached modules, escaped directives, `server-only` imported in the client context and `client-only` in the server context (both Next.js build errors), and inline `"use server"` function bodies. External packages imported in the client context are listed per module. Bundler-specific behavior such as `next/dynamic` with `ssr: false`, conditional exports of installed packages, and runtime selection (`runtime = 'edge'`) are not modeled.

**Validation.** `crates/frameshift-engine/tests/app_router.rs` holds five facade contracts on a fictional project created at test time: segment, pattern, and convention classification; the route list with patterns, chains, slots, private folders, package-level files, and the page-and-route and duplicate-pattern issues; convention exports; render contexts with boundaries, both-context modules, server actions, type-only erasure, and every issue kind; and a project without a root layout and with an ignored directory. The ignored oracle compares 5,746 generated app-relative paths, over thirteen directory shapes, seventeen names, and thirteen extensions with two extension sets, with the matcher and `normalizeAppPath` of the pinned Next.js 16.4.2 distribution, installed outside the repository with install scripts disabled; it executes only those pure functions:

```text
FRAMESHIFT_NEXT_MODULE=<external next package directory> cargo test -p frameshift-engine --offline --locked --test app_router external_next_js_route_oracle -- --ignored --nocapture
```

All cases agree; removing the digit variant of icon names produced 312 differences. Conventions that the app loader resolves by exact name (`template`, `loading`, `error`, the access fallbacks, and the root-only files) are outside the matcher and are covered by the facade contracts. Next.js 14 and 15 lack some conventions (`forbidden` and `unauthorized` arrived in 15.1, `global-not-found` in 15.4, `proxy` in 16); the tree reports them for every version, and version-specific support is the knowledge base's concern. `pageExtensions` from `next.config.*` is a parameter; the default is `tsx`, `ts`, `jsx`, `js`.

## 41. Static evaluator and experiment E3 (`D-092`)

Implemented as the fourth block of `D-092` in `crates/frameshift-evaluate`, exposed as `frameshift_engine::evaluate`. It evaluates a safe subset of JavaScript and TypeScript and never executes source: everything outside the subset is a `Value::Unknown` with a `Reason` and the module and span where it arose.

**Values.** `Value` is `undefined`, `null`, a boolean, a `Number` (an `f64` compared by bits, formatted as `Number::prototype.toString` does), a BigInt by its spelling, a string, an array (holes are `undefined`), an `Object` (own properties in JavaScript own-key order, with a `complete` flag), or `Unknown`. `is_known()` requires every part to be known and every object complete; `unknowns()` lists the unknown parts. Reasons are `Unsupported`, `Call` (with the callee's name or member-chain spelling), `Function`, `RegExp`, `Environment` (`process.env`, with the variable name), `Global`, `Dynamic`, `Mutable`, `Package` (an import from a package, never read), `Unresolved` (with the resolver's reason), `NotExported`, `Syntax`, `Cycle`, `DepthLimit`, `WorkLimit`, and `ValueLimit`.

**Subset.** Numeric (including legacy octal and separators), BigInt, string, boolean, and `null` literals; template literals with known substitutions, using the cooked values that `SyntaxTree::template_cooked` decodes (syntax crate); array literals with spreads of arrays and strings; object literals with static, computed, and shorthand keys and spreads, where methods are `Function` values, getters and setters `Unsupported`, and a literal `__proto__` key or an unknown spread makes the object incomplete; parentheses and the TypeScript `as`, `satisfies`, non-null, and angle-bracket assertions; `!`, `typeof`, `void`, `+`, `-`, and `~`; arithmetic, string concatenation, bitwise, shift, comparison, strict and loose equality on primitives; `&&`, `||`, and `??` with short-circuiting on a known left side; conditionals with a known test; member access on known objects, arrays (`length` and indexes), and strings (`length` and UTF-16 indexes), including optional chaining; `undefined`, `NaN`, and `Infinity`. Calls, `new`, assignments, functions, classes, regular expressions, `in`, `instanceof`, and the comma operator are reasons. `process.env` and its properties are `Environment`.

**Bindings and modules.** An identifier resolves through binding analysis (section 39). A binding with one `const`, `let`, or `var` declaration and a direct name or one property of an object pattern (`const { a, b: c = 1 } = init`, with its default) evaluates its initializer, but only if no reference writes it and none may mutate its value: a property write, update, deletion, or method call on a member chain from it, passing it to a call or `new`, a JSX attribute, or an assignment into a property are mutations or escapes, and an alias into another binding, also through an array or object literal, requires that binding to be mutation-free too (to a depth of eight). `module.exports = value` reads its value. Imports resolve with `snapshot_resolver`, the resolver that the module graph builds (`frameshift-modules`, which now also exposes `source_kind`), and evaluate the imported export of a local module, including JSON modules as their parsed value; re-exports, `export *` delegation for named exports, TypeScript `export =`, and CommonJS `module.exports` (when it is the module's only use of the global `module` and `exports`) are followed. `Evaluator::config` returns the default export, else `export =` or `module.exports`. `Evaluator::detached` evaluates trees that a caller supplies through `tree_export` and resolves no imports. Mutations of a module's values by other modules are not detected.

**Limits.** Three entries bring `Limits::entries` to 56: `evaluate.max-work` (16,777,216 charged units: nodes visited, source bytes read, and value sizes built or copied), `evaluate.max-depth` (64 nested binding and module evaluations), and `evaluate.max-value-size` (1,048,576: string bytes plus one per element and property). Values are at most 128 levels deep and expressions are followed to 256 levels, so recursive helpers are bounded. Exhaustion yields `WorkLimit`, `DepthLimit`, or `ValueLimit` values, not errors; cached values are shared by one evaluator, so only a reached limit makes results depend on request order.

**Validation.** `crates/frameshift-engine/tests/evaluate.rs` holds four facade contracts: module configurations with literals, templates, number formatting, computed keys, own-key order, and environment variables; CommonJS, TypeScript, JSON, re-export chains, and spreads of calls; calls, functions, packages, every mutation and escape form, destructuring with defaults, and unwritten `let`; and cycles, value and work limits, missing exports, and missing modules. Two unit tests check number formatting and conversions. The `evaluate` fuzz target (section 7) checks every tree that parses; an instrumented 60-second smoke run on macOS arm64 completes 616,430 executions, reaches 24,592 features, and retains 5,035 inputs without findings, which is smoke evidence, not the CPU-hour budget of `D-044`.

**Experiment E3 (2026-10-24).** The opt-in `external_config_evaluation` test evaluates every `next.config.*`, `tailwind.config.*`, and `postcss.config.*` file of the directories in `FRAMESHIFT_E3_DIRS`, with each file's directory as the package, and prints its class and the reasons of its unknown parts:

```text
FRAMESHIFT_E3_DIRS=<dir>[;<dir>...] cargo test -p frameshift-engine --release --offline --locked --test evaluate external_config_evaluation -- --ignored --nocapture
```

The twelve repositories of the approved `D-043` sample were fetched again at their pinned commits (only `examples/` of `vercel/next.js`) into a cache outside the repository and only read. They hold 530 configuration files:

| Kind | Files | Fully known | Partially known objects | Objects without a known property | Unknown | Known top-level properties |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `next.config.*` | 207 | 124 (59.9%) | 14 | 19 | 50 | 106 of 146 (72.6%) |
| `tailwind.config.*` | 140 | 61 (43.6%) | 77 | 0 | 2 | 293 of 373 (78.6%) |
| `postcss.config.*` | 183 | 181 (98.9%) | 0 | 0 | 2 | 181 of 181 (100%) |

Unknown parts by reason: for Next.js configurations, 53 calls (mostly plugin wrappers such as `withX(config)` and `require`), 26 functions (`webpack`, `headers`, `redirects`, and whole configurations exported as functions), 7 environment variables, 4 globals (all `__dirname`), and 1 regular expression; for Tailwind, 89 calls (nearly all `require` of plugins and themes), 2 package values, 3 unresolved imports, and 2 empty test fixtures without exports; for PostCSS, 2 unresolved imports. Every unresolved import traces to a resolver refusal, not an evaluator defect: four packages use `"moduleResolution": "node"`, which the profile of section 37 rejects, so their resolutions are configuration evidence, and one PostCSS configuration re-exports a sibling package's file outside its package. The review of every non-call reason found no evaluation defect; it led to support for object destructuring, which turns `const { fontFamily } = require(...)` into a `Call` reason rather than `Unsupported`.

**E3 result.** Plain object configurations are statically evaluable, and nearly all remaining unknown values are explicit calls, functions, or environment inputs, which the safe subset must not execute. The owner accepted `P-018` (DECISIONS.md) on this evidence: configuration rules assess known properties individually and report every unknown part with its reason. In the original E3 measurement, older projects with `"moduleResolution": "node"` lost local resolution entirely; `D-093` subsequently implements that lookup as a separate Node10 profile (section 42), before M5. The table above remains the original E3 measurement.


## 42. Node10 local resolution profile (`D-093`)

`ResolutionConfig::profile()` exposes `ResolutionProfile::Bundler` (the existing default when absent) or `Node10`. Case-insensitive `moduleResolution` values `node` and `node10` select Node10. Relative configuration inheritance overrides the profile independently of other options and preserves the origin of inherited paths. Explicit Node10 accepts classic output formats (`none`, `amd`, `umd`, `system`) in addition to the existing formats; Node16/NodeNext output formats and unmodeled lookup overrides remain unsupported.

Node10 follows the pinned TypeScript 6.0.3 `nodeModuleNameResolverWorker` with legacy features: a complete TypeScript/declaration pass over relative paths, ordered `paths` alternatives, `baseUrl`, file substitution, and directory entry points, followed by a complete JavaScript/JSON pass. This differs from bundler lookup even with `allowJs: true`: an earlier JavaScript alias target loses to a later typed target. Directory packages read `typings` or `types` in the first pass, otherwise `main`, then index files; the second pass reads `main` and index files, ignoring type fields. Both reference modes select the same lookup in Node10; edge modes still describe the source/emitted import form, not a package condition.

Package `imports`, `exports`, and self-references never participate. A non-relative request can still resolve through `paths` or `baseUrl` before bare dependency classification. Unmapped `#` requests remain `NotFound`; self names remain external dependencies. Installed package contents are never read. Existing exact asset fallbacks, selected-package containment, excluded preferred-target refusal, malformed-manifest evidence, unsupported `typesVersions`, explicit JSON references, snapshot-only I/O, and cumulative work limits remain in force. No dependency or limit is added.

Facade tests cover profile spelling, typed-pass precedence over alias alternatives, base URL and directory indexes, extension substitution, inheritance overrides, excluded candidates, directory field passes, disabled package maps, and configuration evaluation through local imports. Separate opt-in file and package oracles share the existing fictional projects and pinned TypeScript 6.0.3 reference with explicit Node10 options. Measured validation is recorded below.

**Differential and real-world validation (2026-10-24, macOS ARM64).** The pinned resolver agrees on all 7,336 Node10 file/alias cases and 14,964 Node10 package/directory cases, including all nonempty presence combinations of six file candidates for six directory-manifest shapes. The latter resolves 5,628 local targets. The independent bundler comparisons remain unchanged and pass on 4,655 file cases and 10,428 package cases. Node10 comparisons check targets in both import forms; only the bundler comparison checks TypeScript package-map modes, since Node10 has no such mode.

E3 was repeated on the same twelve approved `D-043` checkouts after verifying every pinned commit and clean working tree. All 530 files retain the original known-property and complete-file counts of section 41. Three Tailwind unresolved-configuration reasons now become package values (five package reasons in total): their local imports are resolved, and external packages remain intentionally unevaluated. The two PostCSS refusals remain explained: a package-based TypeScript `extends` is unsupported, and a sibling-package reference leaves the selected package. No sample was installed, built, or executed, and no source or reference package is committed.

**Local checks.** The offline locked debug and release workspace suites, E1 generated corpus contracts, all three E2 goldens, formatting, and all-target Clippy with warnings denied pass. A regression verifies the exact inclusive cumulative work boundary across both Node10 passes. A 120-second instrumented `modules` smoke run completes 1,912,811 executions, reaches 26,542 coverage features, and retains 6,802 inputs without findings. This is smoke evidence, not the CPU-hour budget of `D-044`. Native six-platform tests, packaging, conventions, and automated review run on the PR head.

```text
FRAMESHIFT_TYPESCRIPT_MODULE=<external typescript package directory> cargo test -p frameshift-engine --offline --locked --test module_graph --test package_scopes external_typescript -- --ignored --nocapture
```
